**Hermetic exit command (CI job `epic-17-exit`, no secrets; created by 17-1 AC1, extended by 17-3b AC1 and 17-6 AC1; red until green):**

```
maos run spirits/topologies/worker-sandbox.toml --once                      # 1 — T3 probing fixture (R17-78): open("/host-home/.ssh") and open(<MAOS_HOME>/control.json) → ENOENT/EACCES, connect(127.0.0.1:9) → ECONNREFUSED, connect(1.1.1.1:443) → ENETUNREACH (internet unreachable), and a request for a destination outside the allowlist presented to the in-container forwarder → 403 from the egress proxy; three refusals journaled as kind-8 sandbox.block rows (two filesystem, one network)
maos audit query --spirit worker --format plain                             # 1b — the three kind-8 rows: two filesystem rows from the fixture's structured stderr (a self-report from inside the sandbox that proves the mount set — not a kernel control), one network row from the egress proxy's confirmed journal write before it returns the 403 (the control) — never from echoed stdout
cargo build --release -p maos-bin -p maos-wasm-host --features maos-bin/wasm-host --bin maos --bin maos-wasm-runner && target/release/maos run examples/example-spirit-ts/manifest.toml --once   # 2 — 17-3c (R7): the TS Spirit (forms=["wasm-component"], schema 5, artifact=dist/spirit.wasm built by componentize-js in the same job — 17-3b) is admitted, launched under its admitted T2 SandboxSpec and handles frames on the IAC bus under its own spirit_pid; release profile mandatory (R17-38: debug Component::new 10,506 ms > the 10 s watchdog → exit 3)
maos audit query --spirit example-spirit-ts --format plain                     # 2b — 17-8 AC1 (R17-162, re-stating 17-3d): under participant scope the --once seed is a frame the Spirit received, so its recall and its successful fetch of the seed appear as its own rows — exactly R1 log.recall → R2 log.fetch, no refusal row (scripts/epic_17_exit_line2b.py)
cargo test --locked -p maos-wasm-host --test t2_sandbox_kill -- --nocapture   # 3 — T2 for real (17-6 AC1; run by `wasm-host-tests` on the four-image matrix, identical command): the forbidden-syscall probe dies of SIGSYS (31) on ptrace, `/bin/true` and the Rust benign probe exit 0 under the same spec, and the ungranted `cat` is refused by Landlock on the read; in CI a refused spawn FAILS (rule 11(b)), off CI it prints `SKIP`; `epic-17-exit` (17-1 AC1) carries this line verbatim
cargo test --locked -p maos-bin --features network --test worker_terminal_facts_17_7 -- --nocapture --test-threads=1   # 4 — 17-7 (R12): T3 retained lifecycle, x64 T3 lane in the delegated rootless envelope: an immediate-exit Worker completes; intentional exit(137), an external SIGKILL and a memory.max OOM journal their own task.orphaned rows; a panicking or over-budget on_unload leaves no running container and no unreported residue; exact rows fixed in the 17-7 story AC1
```

**Exit-block provenance (dry-parsed at `9f920180`; `bash -n` clean):**
- Line 1 — `maos run <path> --once` exists (`crates/maos-bin/src/worker_spawn.rs:44-77` `parse_run_args`, accepts `<manifest> [--live] [--once]`; hand-parser fallthrough `main.rs:1715,1733-1739`); `[[topology.spirits]]` parsing exists (`crates/maos-bin/src/topology.rs:57-68`; an entry carries only `manifest`/`path`/`host`, so `egress` lives in the manifest, never on the entry). `spirits/topologies/worker-sandbox.toml` **created by 17-1 AC1** (`ls spirits/topologies/` = 5 files, none a Worker sandbox @`0d73cac3`); the probing fixture `spirits/worker/src/bin/worker-probe-fixture.rs` **created by 17-1 AC1** (`ls spirits/worker/src/bin/` = `worker-cli-fixture.rs` only, "no network, no filesystem" by design, `:22`); the new fixture is a **static** binary and needs an admission path (`select_worker_cli` `worker_cli.rs:838-848` knows only `worker-cli-fixture|codex|claude`; built-in host grant `worker_spawn.rs:157-163`; the `--maos-bridge-probe` handshake — R17-75/R17-77). Job-step preconditions, all secret-free but **none of them exists yet** (R17-13, R17-76, R17-77): podman (`t3-smoke-busybox` install step `discipline.yml:1713-1716`), `MAOS_T3_RUNTIME` (`runtime_detect.rs:2,39`), and a test-key `t3-image.lock` + `MAOS_T3_IMAGE_TRUST_ANCHOR_PUB_HEX` (`image_verify.rs:17-27`; `image_lock.rs:16-19,61-75` reads `MAOS_T3_IMAGE_LOCK_PATH`) — the shipped lock is a placeholder `is_shipped_placeholder` refuses (`image_lock.rs:187-195`), no lock-signing tool exists, and `spawn_t3` compares the attested sha to the runtime's RepoDigests (`spawn.rs:79-93`), so the job originates a keypair, pulls a base image **by digest**, and signs a lock; `nfr-sec-1-t3-image-signature` (`:1692-1705`) sets none of it. The base-image pull is network-for-dependencies, precedented, no secret. **red at HEAD:** the topology file is missing and no `epic-17-exit` job exists; the Worker is granted T3 and spawned bare (`crates/maos-kernel-core/src/lifecycle/cli_wrapper/runtime.rs:523-526`), so no errno is produced and no kind-8 row exists.
- Line 1b — `maos audit query [--spirit <name>] [--format ndjson|plain] [--plain]` exists (`main.rs:1668-1703`; `--spirit` resolves a name to the latest (boot_nonce, spirit_pid), `maos-shell/src/lib.rs:184-196`, so every kind-8 row must carry the Worker's `spirit_pid`). **red at HEAD:** zero kind-8 rows for `worker` — the producers are `CapAuditEvent::SandboxBlock` (`crates/maos-kernel-core/src/capability/cap_audit/writer_task.rs:101-116`) and, since 17-3c, the bridge's own confirmed-write fallback (`runtime.rs:704-713`, T2 SIGSYS/OOM only); neither is reached from a container-internal error, and `emit_t3_escape_block` (`t3/cap_audit_bridge.rs:14-27`) has zero callers (R17-83).

**Confidence rules (ratified 2026-09-04 R2, binding):** 1 verb-first (exit verbs exist at HEAD or are AC1 in this epic; `check-exit-commands`) · 2 hermetic exit, live optional (paid/human/clock legs are operator-lane rows) · 3 foundations first · 4 cite-or-cut (every AC names file:line + kernel-Δ) · 5 spike before build · 6 size from measurement (+30%) · 7 one decision per fork · 8 inventory from the tree · **10 route by charter, never by bucket** (Epic-15 retrospective 2026-09-12, operator-ratified): a deferral names one of three destinations and never a default one — (a) **fix at origin** when the item is inside the story's own blast radius (the eased CEILING RULE removes the budget reason this was declined); (b) **the story that already touches that file**, which is the first question asked, not the last; (c) **a theme story (20-3a/20-3b, 21-4) only when the item matches that story's charter.** Rationale: Epic 14 closed early because nine of its eleven remaining rows were gates, registries, ledgers and ceilings; the Epic-15 retrospective measured this lane repeating it, with two items filed to `21-4` that had nothing to do with one instrument and the env registry · **9 refine in the retro** (operator directive 2026-09-06, binding): this epic's retrospective is convened as a refinement session, not a write-up — it re-preflights the NEXT epic against the tree at that day's HEAD (citations, exit-block dry-parse, foundations, budget) and files the OLD→NEW edits before that epic's first story is created; a refinement round may also be convened mid-epic when a story disproves a premise. `epic-N-retrospective` is therefore `backlog`, never `optional`.

**Confidence rule 11 — a control is real, or it is labelled** (operator-ratified 2026-09-25 with the directive *real behaviour, not green runs* — and *not too strict*; binding from the next story created): it governs **controls** only — code or CI that refuses, contains, detects or attests (sandbox tiers, capability and egress checks, audit and receipt writers, gates); ordinary features and unit tests are untouched. (a) **Wired:** a doc, a *Closes* line or an AC claims a control only once a production path calls it; the control may land earlier if the story that wires it is a named, not-done tracker key. (b) **Ran:** in CI a control's test runs or fails — a skip, `continue-on-error` or `|| echo` fails the job on a runner declared capable of the control; skipping stays legal locally and on a runner the job declares incapable, and always prints a named reason. A job may stay **ADVISORY** only with an owner and a reason in its YAML, and an advisory job is never cited as proof. (c) **Proven red once:** when a control lands or changes, a planted fault turns it red — seen in CI with the run id recorded, or run locally with the command and output committed when CI cannot host the substrate; it is not re-run on every push. (d) **No big-bang:** an existing control comes under the rule when a story touches it (rule 10(b)) or through `20-3a-gate-honesty`'s inventory.

**Dev-gate:** external holds (NFR-Sec-7, NFR-Comp-1) = GA ledger only. **Model/review:** frontier allowlist + §A6 net binding; non-degradable on stories marked ◆.

### 17-1-worker-egress-allowlist-and-scoped-credential — Worker egress proxy, allowlist and scoped credential ◆ FLAG-Winston

*⚠ R8 (2026-09-29, 17-3b creation):* AC5's rewrite of `docs-site/docs/cookbook/cli-wrapper-spirit.md` (+ ko) also **adds that page to `crates/maos-bin/tests/cookbook_manifests_17_3b.rs`** (17-3b excludes it; R17-64). If 17-3b lands first, schema 5 already exists — AC2 adds `[cli_wrapper].egress` to v5 with no second bump (a new `abi-ratifications.toml` entry for the field only).

*⚠ R9 (2026-10-06, 17-1 creation, HEAD `0d73cac3`; story `implementation-artifacts/17-1-worker-egress-allowlist-and-scoped-credential.md`, decision record `17-1-flag-winston-decision.md`):* twenty premises of the text below were re-measured and are rewritten in place; the OLD→NEW rows are **R17-70…R17-89 in §R9**. Headline, in order of cost: (R17-75) *applies-or-refuses T3, never bare* moves every hermetic Worker test (≈12 files and the xtask `demo_j1`) onto podman or a typed refusal — every `[cli_wrapper]` Worker is granted exactly T3; (R17-76/R17-77) there is no `epic-17-exit` job, `t3-smoke-busybox` runs a test target that does not exist, `t3-escape-corpus` executes nothing, and no T3 test image, lock signer or forwarder exists; (R17-78) exit line 1's `connect(127.0.0.1:9)` never reaches the proxy, so the network row is sourced from a forwarded request; (R17-73) under T3 the container's environment is its `--env NAME` flags, not `env_clear()` on the `Command` (that clears the podman client); (R17-70) the kernel-Δ list loses `security/manifest.rs` (0 lines) and gains a measured figure; (R17-84) the aggregate KLOC hard-fail (+450 free) binds before any per-crate row. Two 17-3c deferred rows are folded in: `deferred-work.md:1072` (AC3(a)) and `:1076` (AC4(d)).

*Closes · Δ:* S2 (Workers) · FR4/FR5 (FR47 as narrowed by ADR-061) · kernel-Δ **measured +132 `src_lines` / +102 tokei (or +60 / +45 with D-6) — grant ratified 2026-10-06 (variant B, below)** over `runtime.rs` +67, `t3/spawn.rs` +23, `t3/argv.rs` +14, `admission.rs` +27, `lifecycle.rs` +1 (`security/manifest.rs` = 0, R17-70), re-pin `kernel-core-baseline.toml`, `kloc.toml:255`, `RATIFIED_AT_EASING` and the surface pin in the same commit · out-of-kernel: egress proxy + bearer table + in-container forwarder in a **new leaf crate `maos-egress`** +300–600 (its own 15-2 row, today a phantom `kloc.toml:638`; workspace 57 → 58), maos-bin wiring within §E's +200–400 envelope (`worker_spawn.rs`, `supervision.rs`, `worker_cli.rs`, `main.rs`), maos-manifest +40–80 (the field itself measured +2), maos-domain +4 (two admission-error variants; zero headroom), xtask (the T3 test-lock generator, the ADR-061 anchor)

*Ruled 2026-10-06:* grant variant B (≤ +60 `src_lines` / ≤ +45 tokei) ratified; ADR-061 amendment ratified; Q3–Q7 ruled as recommended (story §Operator questions). Story `ready-for-dev`.

*⚠ R10 (2026-10-07, frontier re-verification at `d371b5a4`):* rows **R17-90…R17-104 in §R10**. Headline: AC2's *N-1: a manifest below schema 5 declaring `egress` is a typed refusal* cannot be implemented — a `[cli_wrapper]` manifest has no `manifest_schema_version` (R17-90, story Q8); shipped host grants name bare hosts while AC2's grammar is `host:port` (R17-93, Q9); the ruled kernel ceiling has zero slack against the prototype (R17-98, Q10); the aggregate has **+129** free, not +450 (R17-91); AC2's *three pins* are eleven (R17-94); AC5 misses five published sites and the error page is hand-maintained (R17-100); `macos-check` (20-6) and `check-model-currency` (17-5) now bind the diff (R17-101).

*⚠ R10 rulings (2026-10-07):* **R17-105** supersedes AC2's N-1 sentence (no schema gate; `deny_unknown_fields` on `[cli_wrapper]` lands here); **R17-106** sets the destination type and grammar (AC2) and the upstream scheme; **R17-107** extends 17-1's kernel charter deletion-only (two dead T3 emitters) and routes FR13 quarantine semantics to `20-1`; **R17-108** adds to AC4 the refusal of stdin-keeping postures under T3. AC5's site list grows by the cookbook stdin claim and the manifest-reference pages (story AC5).

*Design in force (D-B, ADR-061 authored by 15-5; the M3 amendment of 17-3a Q6 RATIFIED 2026-10-06 — ADR-061 `## Amendment — Story 17-1`):* the Worker container keeps `--network=none` toward the internet; its only reachable endpoint is a **daemon-owned Unix socket bind-mounted at `/proxy/egress.sock`** (0666 inside a daemon-owned 0700 directory) behind which a daemon-side reverse proxy holds the vendor key, enforces the manifest ∩ host-grant allowlist, and journals every refusal before it returns it; an in-container forwarder serves `127.0.0.1:18080` for the CLI's base URL (ADR-061's *on host loopback* cannot hold — a `--network=none` container cannot reach host loopback by construction, R17-45). The child environment carries a per-spawn, **daemon-minted** proxy-scoped bearer (ADR-061 `:68-73` never says *kernel*; the mint is composition-root code in maos-bin over a `maos-egress` table — R17-85) — never the raw key. The production spawn is `spawn_child`'s T3 arm calling a piped `spawn_t3` (`runtime.rs:523-526` spawns T3 bare today, inside 17-6's `spawn_child`; the Worker is admitted as T3 at `worker_spawn.rs:644-669`), so the Worker is under T3 for the first time. Order is the ADR's: proxy and bearer first, the T3 route second, `env_clear` and the guard inversion in the same commit as the route — never standalone.

- **AC1 (command).** Exit lines 1 and 1b run in the new **`epic-17-exit`** discipline job, which this AC **creates** (no `epic-*-exit` job exists — `grep -nE 'epic-1[0-9]-exit' .github/workflows/discipline.yml` = 0 @`0d73cac3`, R17-76) as a matrix `ubuntu-24.04` + `ubuntu-26.04` (x64 only for T3, D6/R17-50; template `spikes/story-17-3a-wasm-recall/egress/ci-probe.yml.txt:154-171`; never `ubuntu-latest`, which both T3 jobs use today, `discipline.yml:1675,1708`). It also **carries exit lines 2 (17-3c) and 3 (17-6) verbatim**, as the header requires (line 3 additionally stays in the four-image `wasm-host-tests`, `:3017-3055`; line 2's CI proof today runs inside `wasm-host-tests` via `scripts/prove_17_3c.py`, `:3138-3152`, never as the verbatim line). Preconditions the job **originates** (R17-13, R17-77): podman (`t3-smoke-busybox` step `:1713-1716`), `MAOS_T3_RUNTIME`, a **test Ed25519 keypair**, a base image **pulled by digest**, a signed test `t3-image.lock` (`MAOS_T3_IMAGE_LOCK_PATH`) and `MAOS_T3_IMAGE_TRUST_ANCHOR_PUB_HEX` — the shipped lock is a placeholder `image_lock.rs:187-195` refuses and no signer exists, so the generator is built here (xtask subcommand or test support; kernel lines 0). This AC creates `spirits/topologies/worker-sandbox.toml` (two `[[topology.spirits]]` entries; `egress` is a manifest key, never an entry key — `topology.rs:57-68`) and the probing fixture `spirits/worker/src/bin/worker-probe-fixture.rs` (`[[bin]]` beside `worker-cli-fixture`, `spirits/worker/Cargo.toml:11-13`; **statically linked** so any digest-pinned base image runs it; admitted through `select_worker_cli`, the built-in host grant and the `--maos-bridge-probe` handshake, R17-75). It holds open ≥ 3 s after its probes so `inspect_container_host_pid`'s retry window (≈2.25 s, `t3/spawn.rs:158-189`) sees a running container (R17-72). Under T3 (`--user=65534:65534 --read-only --network=none`, only `/maos/spirit:ro` and the egress socket bind-mounted — `t3/argv.rs:72-78`) the fixture's `open("/host-home/.ssh")` fails `ENOENT`/`EACCES` — and so does its `open` of `<MAOS_HOME>/control.json`, the operator door's bearer file (Story 16-1 round-table F-D: a bare Worker runs as the operator uid, so mode `0600` does not protect it); `connect(127.0.0.1:9)` → `ECONNREFUSED` and `connect(1.1.1.1:443)` → `ENETUNREACH` (**internet unreachable**, R17-45); and a request for a destination outside the allowlist, presented to the in-container forwarder, is refused `403` by the proxy. **Corrected (R17-78):** the direct `connect(127.0.0.1:9)` never reaches the proxy — `127.0.0.1` inside a `--network=none` container is the container's own loopback — so it is a self-reported errno, **not** a network `sandbox.block` row; the network row comes from the proxy's refusal of the forwarded request. Exactly **three** kind-8 rows result for `worker`: two filesystem (derived from the fixture's structured stderr marker by the bridge's confirmed-write fallback in `pump_to_journal` — a self-report from inside the sandbox that proves the mount set, not a kernel control) and one network (journaled by the proxy through the confirmed write `TransparencyLogAdapter::insert_frame_event`, `transparency_log.rs:612`, which panics on failure — I2 — **before** the 403 is written: ADR-061 `:63-66`, R17-33; never `emit_sandbox_block`'s fire-and-forget `try_send`, `security/mod.rs:504-520`). None may come from the `CliSubprocessOutput` (kind 21) echo (`runtime.rs:807-816`). The probing Worker must be the **first** binding of its root so its id is `worker` (`supervision.rs:1481-1490`, R17-9). A second entry with `egress = ["127.0.0.1:<echo>"]` (inside the host grant) reaches a hermetic echo listener through the proxy (positive control) and writes no kind-8 row. Which sandbox produces which errno is stated in the story: all of the above are T3 outcomes; under T2 the dial would die with `EPERM` (no `socket`/`connect` in the seccomp allow-list, `security/sandbox/linux.rs:262-329`) and `~/.ssh` would be `EACCES` under Landlock (`linux.rs:178-260`) — T2 is fixture-only for Workers (AC2).
- **AC2.** `[cli_wrapper]` gains `egress = ["host:port", …]` (`maos-manifest` `CliWrapperConfig`, `manifest.rs:4255-4279`; schema 5 already exists, so **no bump** — an `abi-ratifications.toml` entry on the `17-3c-iac-send-capability` model `:106-124`, `manifest_field_coverage` rows (`crates/maos-kernel-core/tests/manifest_field_coverage.rs:33-75`; none exist for `cli_wrapper`, nor `tests/fixtures/manifest/cli_wrapper/`) and a validator for the `host:port` grammar: lower-case host or IPv4 literal, port 1–65535, no wildcard, path, userinfo or duplicate). `CliWrapperConfig` is **not** `deny_unknown_fields` (R17-80), so at HEAD a manifest declaring `egress` loads with the field silently dropped; N-1 is therefore **refuse** — a manifest below schema 5 that declares `egress` is a typed refusal (D-5). Adding the pub field breaks nine struct-literal sites (two are kernel `src`: `admission.rs:326`, `lifecycle.rs:66`). **The manifest requests; the host grants** (R17-79): `HostGrant.permitted_egress_destinations` (`maos-domain/src/host_grant.rs:35-38`, loaded from `MAOS_HOST_GRANTS`, printed today as `"egress":"declared-not-enforced"` with `EGRESS_ENFORCEMENT_FOLLOWUP`, `worker_spawn.rs:151,512-532`) becomes the grant ceiling — `manifest.egress ⊆ grant.permitted_egress_destinations` or a typed `EEgressNotGranted` refusal; the record flips to `enforced`, the follow-up id is retired, and its three pins flip in the same commit (`maos-cli/src/subcommands.rs:3126` + test `:5228-5238`; `xtask/src/demo_j1.rs:63-64`, `xtask/tests/demo_j1_tests.rs:265`). `admit_cli_wrapper_journaled` (`admission.rs:188`) refuses a manifest whose `egress` is non-empty when no proxy is configured (`EEgressProxyUnavailable`, kernel `check_egress`); `ci_default_guard`'s `network_requested` bool (`runtime.rs:217-243`) has **no production caller** (R17-81) and is deleted with its three unit tests and the `cli_wrapper/mod.rs:41` re-export if the operator takes D-6, else the "gets its manifest source" claim is withdrawn. The Worker container keeps `--network=none` and reaches ONLY the daemon-owned egress socket (M3: `--volume=<socket>:/proxy/egress.sock` joins `t3/argv.rs:78`'s volume; `t3/argv.rs` is a podman command line with no allowlist flag and rootless podman cannot program nftables, so the proxy is the only single mechanism satisfying AC2 and AC3; M1/M2 are rejected — both reach the internet, R17-45). The proxy (`maos-egress`) is a reverse proxy, one listener per spawn in a daemon-owned 0700 directory, socket 0666, routing `/<host>:<port>/<path>` to an admitted upstream, with bounded header/body sizes and timeouts, streaming pass-through, upstream TLS owned by the proxy, and **every refusal journaled through a confirmed write before the response is returned** (R17-33); the crate has no `maos-kernel-core` edge — the journal sink is a trait object injected by maos-bin (ADR-061 `:87-89`). T2 Workers cannot dial at all (no `socket`/`connect` in the seccomp allow-list) and are stated as fixture-only.
- **AC3.** (a) **Every non-container spawn clears its environment.** `spawn_and_bridge` calls `cmd.env_clear()` at its single `Command` build (`runtime.rs:548-556`) and then applies only `spec.env` — which also closes the 17-3c deferred row (`deferred-work.md:1072`) for the T2 WASM runner (its `spec.env` is `vec![]` and it reads no env). (b) **The T3 container's environment is its `--env` flags** (R17-73): podman never forwards the client's environment, so `env_clear()` on the T3 `Command` clears the *podman client*, not the Worker. `spawn_t3` therefore emits `--env=NAME` (the name only, never `NAME=VALUE` — `/proc/<pid>/cmdline` is readable by the operator uid) for each `spec.env` pair, passes the values in the client's environment, adds `--http-proxy=false` (podman otherwise copies the client's proxy variables in), and runs the client with `env_clear()` plus an explicit allowlist — `PATH`, `HOME`, `XDG_RUNTIME_DIR`, `DBUS_SESSION_BUS_ADDRESS` (what rootless podman needs; confirmed at dev by the `/proc` test). (c) The environment carries `worker_cli.nonsecret_env()` (`worker_cli.rs:134,655`; consumed at `worker_spawn.rs:634`) plus the CLI's base-URL variable pointed at `http://127.0.0.1:18080/<host>:<port>` and a per-spawn, **daemon-minted proxy-scoped bearer** (ADR-061 `:68-73`: audience = this proxy, identity = the Worker's `spirit_pid`/id, destination set, bounded lifetime; not the wire `CapabilityToken`; 32 CSPRNG bytes, stored only as a hash in the `maos-egress` table, constant-time compared, revoked at `finish`/guard drop, never journaled and never in `nonsecret_env`, which `worker_completion_2a.rs:52-65` pins). The bearer rides the CLI's own credential variable name (`ANTHROPIC_API_KEY` / `CODEX_API_KEY`, `worker_cli.rs:668,722`); the raw vendor key is read by the proxy only — `credential_env_var()` (`worker_cli.rs:148,668,722`, zero call sites in `crates/**`) gets its first consumer there, and the FILENAME-control note (`worker_spawn.rs:607-613`) and its comments (`:575-581`, `worker_cli.rs:120-136`) are rewritten to what ships. Base-URL adapters are new (`grep -rn 'base_url\|BASE_URL' crates/maos-bin` = 0): one `WorkerCli` method per vendor CLI. (d) **Inverted in the same commit:** `env_clear_stays_absent_from_the_production_worker_spawn_path` (`crates/maos-cli/tests/credential_posture_2c.rs:299-341`, **six** legs, R17-6; rationale `:6-12` cites the stale 24472 pin) with its rationale rewritten, **and** the ADR-061 anchor that pins its literal (`xtask/tests/decision_adrs_and_provisioning.rs:413-446`, fixture `:1078-1084`) with ADR-061's Context (which must still name `env_clear`); ADR-062 `:161-165` and `README.md:282-284` (*Until Story 17-1 lands…*) are rewritten. ⚠ Story 16-1 round-table F-D (2026-09-13, operator-ratified): `env_clear()` also removes every operator-door variable from the Worker — until this AC lands every Worker inherits the daemon's environment and can call the 16-1 door; the probe fixture asserts, **from inside the container** (it prints its environ keys — under the rootless subuid map `/proc/<host_pid>/environ` is unreadable to the daemon), that **no `MAOS_OPERATOR_`-prefixed key** is present (a prefix match; the door's three names are `main.rs:3164,3167` and `maos-cli/src/door_client.rs:32`) and that no vendor key is.
- **AC4.** `spawn_t3` (`t3/spawn.rs:54`) is the **single Worker path**. (a) *Route.* `spawn_child` (`runtime.rs:513-532`, 17-6's) replaces its `T0 | T3 => cmd.spawn()` arm: T3 calls `spawn_t3`, and a new `BridgeChild::Container(SandboxedContainerChild)` variant adapts the type (R17-5 re-derived: `SpawnedBridge` stores `Option<BridgeChild>`; `spawn_sandboxed` returns `SandboxedChild`; `spawn_t3` returns `SandboxedContainerChild { child: Option<Child>, host_pid, container_name, runtime }`). `spawn_t3` pipes stdin/stdout/stderr (`:115-117`, unpiped today — which is also why the busybox smoke's `wait_with_output` captures nothing) and creates its guard **before** `inspect_container_host_pid`, so a failed inspect no longer leaks the container. `BridgeSpawnSpec` carries no image attestation or container context, so it gains `t3: Option<T3Launch>`; a T3 spec without it is `SandboxRefused`, **never spawned bare**. `spawn_and_bridge` has **two** production callers (`worker_spawn.rs:825`, `spirit_session.rs:263`); the second is T2. From this commit a granted T3 is applied through `spawn_t3` or refused typed (e.g. no container runtime) — matching 17-6 AC5 (D2 = a). `spawn_sandboxed`'s T3 arm (`security/sandbox/mod.rs:170-179`, `SandboxUnavailable`) is unchanged. The Worker's `SandboxSpec.spirit_id`/`from_spirit_id` stop being the literal `"worker"` (`worker_spawn.rs:805,811`) so `--label=maos.spirit_id` is right for `worker-2`. (b) *Pid and kill (R17-4 ruled; R17-72).* The pid the 16-3 observer watches is the **`podman run` client** (`child.get().id()`, `runtime.rs:559`): attached, it is the process whose death ends the Worker, and it is the daemon's child so `waitid(EXITED|NOWAIT)` works (`supervision.rs:2383-2416`); the container's host pid (`inspect`) is informational and not a child (`ECHILD`). Killing only the client leaves the container, so `BridgeChild::kill` also runs `<runtime> kill <name>` and the guard's drop stops and removes it; `crates/maos-bin/src/supervision.rs` joins the Δ line (`:1852-1858` and `:2114-2117` already say *process-tree teardown: 17-1*); `commit_spawn_report` (`spawn.rs:142`, zero callers) is wired so the Worker SCB's `sandbox_tier` is true (16-3 §11 row 9; `deferred-work.md:959`); the `permitted == requested` tier-denial message (`host_grant.rs:134-138`; `:960`) is fixed. (c) *CI.* `t3-smoke-busybox` (`discipline.yml:1708-1725`) drops `continue-on-error` and `|| echo` — **and its test target `sandbox_t3_spawn_linux` does not exist** (R17-76: `crates/maos-kernel-core/tests/` has no such file), so this AC authors it against a verified test image (zero kernel lines); `t3-escape-corpus` (`:1675-1690`) drops the same pair, and since `t3_escape_corpus.rs` only validates the 25 fixtures' JSON shape and executes nothing, this AC adds an executor for the five `--network=none` scenarios (the 17-3a matrix: host reachable through the socket, internet unreachable, socket 0600 refused / 0666 connects) and labels the other twenty honestly (rule 11(a)/(b)); both jobs move off `ubuntu-latest` to the two-image matrix and stay in `aggregate.needs` (`:4511-4513`). (d) *Bridge hardening routed from 17-3c.* `read_newline_delimited` (`runtime.rs:366-376`, the unbounded call `:368`) is bounded by `MAX_FRAME_BYTES`; a longer line is a typed `InvalidData` framing error, never a truncation (`deferred-work.md:1076`). R17-22 (bridge teardown) is **measured, not designed**: re-run the grandchild-on-inherited-stdio probe under `spawn_t3`, where a container kill takes its grandchildren — if the defect dissolves for T3 it closes with the measurement; a bounded drain rides this commit's Δ only if it does not. `select_worker_cli` runs on the manifest command before any wrapping (`worker_spawn.rs:563-568`; `argv_prefix_hash` binds `config.argv_prefix` only, `:701`). (e) *The sweep (R17-75).* Every `[cli_wrapper]` Worker is granted exactly T3, so ≈12 hermetic test files and the xtask `demo_j1` run a fixture Worker bare today; each is triaged to run under podman on a podman-capable runner (skip off CI with a named reason, FAIL on a declared-capable CI runner — rule 11(b)) or, when it tests supervision rather than the Worker, to drive the supervisor against the primitive's T0 path (never a bare `run_cli_wrapper_manifest`). Tests that find Workers by `/proc/<pid>/environ` or ppid (`root_shutdown_unload_16_3.rs:270-400`) are re-keyed — after `env_clear`/T3 they pass vacuously — and `worker_supervision_16_3.rs:1534-1542` SIGKILLs the journaled client pid, not the Worker. `bridge_t2_route_17_6.rs:165-171` (`t3_probe_runs_bare_until_17_1`) is inverted.
- **AC5 (binding — operator decision 2026-09-25, 17-3a RT-9: no hot-fix, so this AC is where the fix cannot be missed; unchanged in force).** The published isolation claims are rewritten to what this story ships, in the commit that lands AC4's re-route, English and Korean together (`check-ko-coverage` presence plus `gate-glossary-lock.js` locked-term counts, `LOCALES.md`): `docs-site/docs/cookbook/cli-wrapper-spirit.md:89` + ko `:90`; `docs-site/docs/deploy/topology.md:110-144,154` (not only `:132-143`: also `:112` *each Spirit runs in its own container*, `:130`, the comparison row `:154` and the `maos run --spirit hello-spirit` example `:143`, which `parse_run_args` refuses) + ko `:111-145,155`. **R17-86: the cookbook manifest block itself is wrong** and is rewritten to the real shape of `spirits/worker/manifest.toml` — it declares both `[class]` and `[cli_wrapper]` (gate 3 `CliWrapperClassConflict`, `maos-bin/src/admission.rs:459-461`, not gate 3b's `CliWrapperUnsupported` that R17-64 expects), `tier = "hardened"` (`:32`) and `[cli_wrapper]` keys (`output_shape_version = 1`, `recovery_policy = "restart"`, `[cli_wrapper.posture] default/allowed_max`) that `CliWrapperConfig` refuses or ignores; its recovery table (restart/fail/ignore) is fictional. The page keeps exactly one ```toml block (`cookbook_manifests_17_3b.rs:49-60`), **is added to that test** (R17-64; ko blocks byte-identical), and the new text names the Worker's real profile — T3 under rootless podman as `t3/argv.rs:72-78` builds it (`--user=65534:65534 --read-only --network=none`, egress only through the proxy) — names only tier values the parser accepts (`T0`–`T4`, `crates/maos-bin/src/worker_spawn.rs:82-92`; there is no `hardened` tier, so following the cookbook today makes `maos run` refuse the manifest with *unknown sandbox tier*), and states what stays unisolated by design (first-party `rust-inproc` runs in the daemon). Also rewritten, because they state the same false claim: `docs/errors/error-catalog.json:85-96` (`ECliWrapperRequiresT3`'s remediation names a field that does not exist and gives a false reason; the generated `docs-site/docs/errors/ECliWrapperRequiresT3.md:22,30,34` is regenerated), `docs-site/docs/understand-maos.md:35` (+ ko `:36`), the `worker_cli.rs:120-136` doc and ADR-062 `:161-165` / `README.md:282-284` (AC3). Review checks it as an AC (R17-34, R17-35, R17-86).

*Landed (2026-10-09, story `17-1-worker-egress-allowlist-and-scoped-credential` → `review`; the epic as amended by §R9/§R10 is the contract, and these notes record what shipped against it):*
- **AC1 — landed.** `epic-17-exit` exists, on a matrix `ubuntu-24.04` + `ubuntu-26.04`, and joins `aggregate.needs`.
  - Lines 1/1b run `spirits/topologies/worker-sandbox.toml` and `maos audit query --spirit worker`, then an independent SQLite read (`scripts/epic_17_exit_line1.py`). Result: exactly three Kernel-origin kind-8 rows for `worker`, zero for the positive control `worker-2`.
  - Line 2 runs inside the 17-3c delegated-cgroup unit, its precondition originated by the job and not muted. Line 3 runs verbatim.
  - Proof run `37988000230` (both images, all jobs green). The line-1 step also refuses a skipping or early-returning runner (Task 10 plant red, run `37999039580`).
- **AC2 — landed as amended by R17-105/-106.**
  - Typed `egress = ["host:port"]`, with `deny_unknown_fields` on `[cli_wrapper]`/`[cli_wrapper.posture]` and no schema gate (schema version → `20-5`).
  - `EgressDestination` lives in `maos-domain` (the grant accepts a bare host ≡ `:443`). Admission enforces manifest ⊆ host grant (`EEgressNotGranted`) and proxy availability (`EEgressProxyUnavailable`).
  - The `maos-egress` proxy sits behind a per-Worker 0666 socket in a private 0700 per-root directory. It verifies a hash-only, pid/Spirit/destination-bound, expiring bearer before any upstream I/O. Upstream is HTTPS, except plain HTTP to a granted IPv4 loopback literal (D-17). Every refusal is journaled by a confirmed `insert_frame_event` before the 403.
  - `maos-egress` is forbidden in the kernel/domain closure (F-10, planted red).
- **AC3 — landed.** `env_clear()` applies to every non-container spawn and to every container-runtime client command (`runtime_command`, extended to `runtime_detect`/`image_verify`/`child` by the 2026-10-09 custody-location ruling). The T3 environment is names-only `--env` plus `--http-proxy=false`. The Worker receives only the scoped bearer, never the raw key; the admission probes clear their environment (D-9; executing the binary on the host → `19-3`).
- **AC4 — landed.**
  - `spawn_t3` is the single Worker path: piped, with the guard created before inspect and a verified launch; a T3 spec without launch data is `SandboxRefused`.
  - Stdin-keeping postures are refused under T3 (D-15), and the reader is bounded at `MAX_FRAME_BYTES`. The dead T3 emitters are deleted (D-16, 98 → 96 kernel files).
  - `t3-smoke-busybox` and `t3-escape-corpus` are unmasked, with the five `--network=none` scenarios executed.
  - The F-6 sweep was migrated onto the signed per-run T3 substrate (capable run `37983335101`).
  - Kernel landed at 25212 `src_lines` / 19513 KLOC (≤ the 25353 / 19564 ceiling).
  - A SIGKILLed T3 Worker is recorded as `exited(code=137)`, per operator ruling A (2026-10-09). Authoritative OOM/signal facts belong to `17-7` (re-homed from 17-2 by D-17-2-FW-07, 2026-10-11; R17-113).
- **AC5 — landed.** Every site of R17-86/R17-100 was rewritten in English and Korean: cookbook, topology, deploy index, understand-maos, manifest v5 `[cli_wrapper]` section plus the v2/v3/index corrections, error catalog and pages, `worker_cli.rs`, ADR-061/062, README and `4-kernel-design.md`. The cookbook page joins `cookbook_manifests_17_3b.rs` (gate-3b refusal plus the pure Worker load gates). `check-ko-coverage` and glossary lock are green, and docs-site run `37995772747` is green.

### 17-2-worker-cgroups-applied — Worker cgroups applied ◆ FLAG-Winston

*Closes · Δ (authoring-time forecast, superseded by R17-116 — not an allowance):* FR6 · kernel-Δ net ≤+10 (−30..+10: delete the root layout −40..−60, `pids.max` +5, `pids_max` cap +3, `--pids-limit` +2; re-pin in the commit) · maos-domain +5–10 (`LifecycleEvent::ResourceCeilingDowngraded`) · maos-manifest `[resources].pids_max` (field on the v5 schema landed by 17-1)

*Design at epic authoring (§H-3) — HISTORICAL, not executable as written: the creation re-preflight, §R11 and the story supersede its layout, CI recipe, undelegated-job construction and enum shape.* One delegation-compatible layout — `linux.rs`'s own-cgroup `<own>/maos.slice/spirit-<id>` (`security/sandbox/linux.rs:374`); the root layout is deleted; T3 Workers are capped by podman flags, documented as the third mechanism. **CI decision:** delegation is provisioned via `sudo` in the job (hosted runners have `sudo`, `t3-smoke-busybox` precedent) AND a second job proves the typed-downgrade branch — both, not one.

- **AC1 (command).** A discipline job `cgroup-ceiling-delegated` delegates a subtree (`sudo mkdir /sys/fs/cgroup/maos && sudo chown -R "$USER" /sys/fs/cgroup/maos && echo '+cpu +memory +pids' | sudo tee /sys/fs/cgroup/cgroup.subtree_control`) and moves the test process into it (`echo $$ > /sys/fs/cgroup/maos/cgroup.procs`, so `find_writable_cgroup_root` — which reads `/proc/self/cgroup`, `linux.rs:357-370` — resolves the delegated subtree); then for a T2 fixture child `cat /sys/fs/cgroup$(cut -d: -f3 /proc/<pid>/cgroup)/{cpu.max,memory.max,pids.max}` shows the manifest caps, and for a T3 Worker `podman inspect` shows `--cpus/--memory/--pids-limit` derived from `[resources]` incl. the new `pids_max` (`t3/argv.rs:79-81`). A second job `cgroup-ceiling-undelegated` (no delegation step) asserts a `LifecycleEvent::ResourceCeilingDowngraded { reason }` journal row (new variant on `crates/maos-domain/src/invariants/i10.rs:75`, maos-domain +5–10; `SpawnError::CgroupUnavailable` already exists, `sandbox/mod.rs:64`) instead of today's `eprintln!` + `noop()` (`scheduler/resource_ceiling.rs:112-120`).
- **AC2.** One layout: `linux.rs`'s own-cgroup `maos.slice/spirit-<id>` (`create_cgroup_dir` `:372-377`, literal `:374`) with `pids.max` written in `apply_cgroup_limits` (`:379-394`, today cpu/memory only). `resource_ceiling.rs`'s root layout (`/sys/fs/cgroup/maos/spirit-<pid>`, `:104`; non-root → `PermissionDenied` → `noop()`, `:105-120`) and `apply_resource_ceiling` (`:76`; doc `:73-74` "NOT called at v0.3-β"; zero callers outside `tests/cgroup_ceiling_smoke.rs`) are **DELETED, not wired**. ⚠ **R8 (Story 16-4 creation 2026-09-16, §11 row 9):** 16-4's `maos purge` **confirmed this independently** — it had provisionally listed `/sys/fs/cgroup/maos/spirit-*` as a fifth MAOS root to remove and **withdrew it**, because no HEAD build can create it and, even where one exists, a cgroup v2 directory is `rmdir`-only (kernel-generated files cannot be unlinked, so `remove_dir_all` gives `EPERM`), needs write on a root-owned parent, and returns `EBUSY` while populated. So **17-2 owns the only surviving cgroup layout and whether a leak is possible under it**; `maos purge` never removes a cgroup directory and mentions one only as pre-17-2 residue. `pids_max` is added to `ResourceCaps`/`ResolvedCaps` (`crates/maos-manifest/src/manifest.rs:105,121`) and to `[resources]` (field on the v5 schema), and T3's `--pids-limit` stops borrowing `fd_max` (`t3/argv.rs:47-57`, the documented DR-5.5a-9 placeholder). The three mechanisms at HEAD (root layout `resource_ceiling.rs:104`, own-cgroup `linux.rs:374`, podman flags `argv.rs:79-81`) become two, and the story documents the podman flags as the T3 mechanism.
- **AC3.** `cgroup_ceiling_smoke` (`crates/maos-kernel-core/tests/cgroup_ceiling_smoke.rs`) loses `#[ignore]` and the `MAOS_CGROUP_TEST` gate (`:5-6,15-20`), targets the own-cgroup layout with `pids_max` in `ResourceCaps` (`:21-25`), and is enrolled in the `cgroup-ceiling-delegated` job (it is in no job today — `grep -n cgroup .github/workflows/discipline.yml` empty).

**Creation re-preflight (2026-10-10, `b19556ab`; implementation guidance above is superseded where corrected here):** the complete story is `implementation-artifacts/17-2-worker-cgroups-applied.md`. AC1–AC3's observable goals remain binding. The live native implementation already discovers an enabled delegated ancestor, uses unique `spirit-<id>-<daemon-pid>-<sequence>` leaves, places the child in `pre_exec`, and performs bounded guard cleanup; preserve those repairs. Provision an empty delegated domain plus a separate populated process leaf, not the historical root-level shell recipe. Preserve CPU refusal without delegation and extend it to explicit PID caps; prove the resource-only downgrade with a memory/FD-only T2 child. Workers currently discard `[resources]`, T3 CPU percentages are passed as core counts, and FD/PID limits share one slot: the story closes all three production gaps. Schema 5 already exists from 17-3b. The downgrade is an additive unit lifecycle event plus typed payload in the real shared `JournalAdapter`; the similarly named scheduler method drops payload into the Transparency Log and is not the required sink. The old root-layout API is deleted, never wired.

**Measured creation evidence (E16-A7):** `implementation-artifacts/17-2-evidence/` retains a formatted, compiled mechanism prototype. Kernel: 25267 → 25110 physical lines (−157) and 19553 → 19454 tokei code lines (−99), including deletion of the whole 170-line obsolete module. The prototype exercised a real local T2 memory-only fallback and an explicit-PID refusal. Delegated/T3 proof, production routing/journaling and re-pins remain implementation obligations, not claimed complete. Both cgroup jobs require success in the aggregate, because mere `needs` enrollment tolerates skipped jobs today. Removing the smoke's ignore requires provisioning the workspace-suite execution too. T2 proof uses the four pinned images; T3 uses the existing two x64 images.

**Preflight round-table (2026-10-11; operator rulings the same day; §R11):** decision record `implementation-artifacts/17-2-flag-winston-decision.md`.

- **FW-01 charter.** The ratified locations are `security/sandbox/{linux,mod}.rs`, `t3/argv.rs`, `cli_wrapper/runtime.rs` and the `scheduler_loop.rs` literal, plus three deletion-only locations: `scheduler/{resource_ceiling.rs,mod.rs}` and `journal/mod.rs`. The journal deletion keeps the writer guard through the live-index insert (R17-109).
- **FW-02/03 ceilings.** **No raise**; exact figures at landing.
- **FW-07 handoffs.** The OOM/signal and short-lived T3 handoffs are **owned by 17-7**, not by this story (R17-113).
- **Corrected in the story:** the hosted delegation precedent is a system-manager `systemd-run … Delegate=yes`, and its helper must also enable `+pids` (R17-111). The lifecycle oracle no longer claims boot-time SCB rehydration, which does not exist (R17-110).

Rule 11(a): the production caller is 20-1's install (named, not done).

### 17-3c-wasm-spirit-on-the-bus-under-t2 — A WASM Spirit runs on the bus, contained by T2 ◆ FLAG-Winston

*⚠ R8 (2026-09-29, 17-3b creation — read before creating this story):* R17-39(a) (instantiate-time fuel → exit 4) and R17-39(d) (the WIT world comment + `runner.rs:30-37` T2 claim) are **done by 17-3b** (R17-60) — do not redo them; only R17-39(b), the guest diagnostics channel, remains here. 17-3b adds runner exits **2 `IncompatibleWorld`** and **5 `UnrepresentableFrame`** beside 3/4: AC3's *"exit 3 becomes a typed admission refusal"* must map **2, 3 and 5**. AC1 **replaces 17-3b's `WasmLaunchNotBuilt`** (last step of `gate_manifest`) with the launch, and exit line 2's provenance is now this story's (R17-65). **Do not edit `wit/spirit.wit`** (not even a comment) unless the story re-issues all 4 provenance-pinned components and adds a ratified `abi-ratifications.toml` entry for any new package version (R17-66). The registry floor for `wasm-component` is T2 at every tier (R17-61).

*Closes · Δ:* S2 for the third-party form — FR5-by-form, **execution half** · epic exit line 2 · kernel-Δ **FLAG-Winston**, measured by a prototype at creation (E16-A7): `memfd_create` (R17-46), the bridge's ADR-032 frame path (AC2), the kind-8 emission at the primitive (R17-48) · maos-bin (the spawned-class session — zero headroom, exact raise; a new module bumps `SCANNED_SOURCE_FILES`, `cohort_daemon_smoke_13_5c.rs:922`) · maos-wasm-host

*Why a story of its own (R17-54).* No production path spawns a class Spirit: every `LoadedSpiritKind` arm builds an in-process object (`crates/maos-bin/src/main.rs:444-468`, `:4472-4545`, `:4926-5477`), and `spawn_and_bridge` is a CLI bridge — it journals the child's stdout as lossy text, never feeds or decodes an ADR-032 frame, and with `Signals` closes stdin at spawn (`crates/maos-kernel-core/src/lifecycle/cli_wrapper/runtime.rs:487-493`, `:571-633`). The old Design-in-force line (*"the runner's stdio is the ADR-032 bridge … stays byte-identical"*) was false. A WASM Spirit is a long-lived participant on the IAC bus; that session is this story. After 17-6 (the T2 route) and 17-3b (the contract).
*Early-start disposition (2026-10-01):* **Preparation is authorized, not runtime implementation.** The usable predecessor is the verified 17-3b contract/admission and refusal snapshot plus 17-6's four-image T2 evidence; **17-3b's `in-progress` status is not a schedule gate**. Its unchecked kernel-verdict finding is unchanged and closes only after 17-3c AC2 delivers the real framed runner-output-to-authorization proof. The durable decision, evidence symbols, eight-case verdict gate, four-image scope, and FLAG-Winston/grant boundary are in `_bmad-output/implementation-artifacts/17-3c-early-start-flag-winston-decision.md` (D-17-3c-ES-01…05). 17-3c is contract-ready for preparation only — not ready-for-dev or done — and 17-3d remains out of scope.

*Isolated preflight (2026-10-01):* `17-3c-early-start-flag-winston-decision.md` now records the actual kernel oracle (`issue_with_mediation` → `PolicyTable::evaluate`) and required enterprise wrapper. A disposable native T0/WASM T2 raw-frame prototype observed eight kernel verdicts, compiled deny-bypass RED/restored GREEN, local `memfd_create` leave-one-out, and clean dependency closure. Its minimum kernel seam measures **+95 physical src lines / +68 tokei code**, not a complete implementation grant. Production SCB/bus/enterprise binding, shared bounded codec, safe conformance/resource cutover, receipts, and four-image containment remain unproven; no baseline change or status completion follows.

*Closure (2026-10-06; supersedes the historical preparation and review notes above):* **17-3b and 17-3c are `done`.**
- **Review:** the §A6 bmad-code-review resolved every 17-3c finding; four mutation pairs went RED then GREEN, and a fresh Ubuntu25.10 x86_64 AC1 proof passed.
- **17-3b:** its final kernel-verdict finding was independently verified. The gate covers eight admitted native/WASM forms, clean and forged, and a compiled deny-bypass mutation goes RED, then GREEN on restore.
- **Integration:** the candidate was integrated into the original checkout. It lands kernel pin 25293, KLOC kernel 19529 / bin 24600 / IAC 7100 / frame-codec 249, and the approved surface rows, and it replaces `WasmLaunchNotBuilt`.
- **Out of scope:** recall remains 17-3d; the R8 obligations still bind. Source: `implementation-artifacts/17-3c-wasm-spirit-on-the-bus-under-t2.md` §Review Findings.


- **AC1 (command) — epic exit line 2.** `cargo build --release … --features maos-bin/wasm-host --bin maos --bin maos-wasm-runner && target/release/maos run examples/example-spirit-ts/manifest.toml --once`: the TS Spirit is admitted (replacing 17-3b's `WasmLaunchNotBuilt`), launched through `spawn_and_bridge` under the `SandboxSpec` admission grants — `T2` plus `FsRead` on the artifact (17-6 RT-2) — receives frames from the IAC bus and emits frames onto it under its own `spirit_pid`, journaled losslessly through 17-3b's `@2.0.0`; `maos audit query --spirit example-spirit-ts` shows them. Platform acceptance uses the user-accepted completed Ubuntu25.10 x86_64 proof; the earlier four-image requirement is superseded for this story only.
- **AC2 — the spawned-class session and authority observation.** Allocate the scheduler SCB and its `spirit_pid` before launch; bind both that identity and the admission-derived `SandboxSpec` to the runner endpoint, never to guest-supplied bytes. An ADR-032 frame path through the one launch primitive uses one shared length-delimited codec and a frame writer on the bridge (not `write_stdin_line`, which is bench/test support, `runtime.rs:691`); each accepted runner output is attributed to the bound pid, delivered to the selected real authorization decision, and journals its input and terminal allow/refusal verdict. Crash observation and supervision reuse 16-3's `WorkerSupervision` and pidfd, with an NFR-Rel-11 halt receipt on every exit. The eight-case native/WASM clean/forged allow/deny gate counts all decision invocations, fails nonreach/nonvacuity, and is red under a compiled bypass mutation at the actual decision path in a disposable snapshot that preserves staged edits; see D-17-3c-ES-02. Each standalone, topology, and door arm (`main.rs:4877`, `:4446`, `:8603`) states whether it loads a WASM Spirit and refuses typed where it does not.
  **17-3b review handoff — closed 2026-10-06:** The 17-3c admitted-session gate supplies the kernel verdict that the equivalence tests could not, and the non-author review verified it. The finding is checked off and 17-3b is `done`.
- **AC3 — no untrusted code inside the daemon and safe launch (R17-55).** At the preserved refusal baseline, `WasmHostAdapter::resolve_launch` compiles **and instantiates** the component inside the unsandboxed daemon (`crates/maos-wasm-host/src/adapter.rs:80-89` → `conformance.rs:45-51`; instantiation can run guest start code, and a timed-out probe thread keeps running). Conformance moves into the T2 runner, whose exits 2/3/5 become typed admission refusals; the daemon reads only artifact metadata and size. Production launch requires a runner/guest resource boundary that bounds guest return-value allocation before outbound serialization; host-to-runner input bounds and the 16 MiB serialized-output cap do not prove that property. Fix the admission grant, artifact read scope, frame bounds, and runner argv before `spawn_and_bridge`, with no bare fallback. Conformance ownership and the refusal→launch sequence are D-17-3c-ES-03; containment acceptance uses the user-amended completed Ubuntu25.10 x86_64 proof, not the superseded four-image requirement. Recall is not part of this AC.
- **AC4 — contained, and proven so.** `memfd_create` joins the T2 allow-list with its leave-one-out proof (drop → rc 3). A WASI guest cannot issue a raw syscall (R17-56), so the SIGSYS proof is the **runner process's**: a debug-only runner fault-inject feature (the house rule — `not(debug_assertions)` + `compile_error!`) issues `ptrace` after `on-start`, driven with the small Rust echo fixture (a debug compile stays under the 10 s watchdog; the TS component needs release). `maos run` ends `Signaled { signal: 31 }`; a kind-8 `SandboxBlock` row, emitted **at the primitive** (`classify_exit` → `emit_sandbox_block`, `crates/maos-kernel-core/src/security/mod.rs:504`), shows in `maos audit query`; the escape-detector consumer counts it (R17-47, R17-48). The runner's false T2 texts are rewritten (`runner.rs:30-37`, `wit/spirit.wit:216-218`, `crates/maos-host/src/lib.rs:42-45` — R17-34, R17-39d), and an instantiate-time fuel trap exits 4, not 3 (R17-39a).
  **Approved runtime/coverage amendment:** final time/compile guard360 s supersedes the historical10 s guard. Existing local SIGSYS/escape-consumer and memfd omission evidence is retained; extra platform-specific repetition/omission controls are not remaining gates under the user's acceptance of completed Ubuntu25.10 x86_64 testing. Unexecuted controls are not claimed passed.

### 17-3d-wasm-log-recall — `log.recall` for WASM Spirits, mediated in-band on the runner's stdio ◆ FLAG-Winston

*⚠ R8 (2026-09-29):* `maos:spirit@2.1.0` is a WIT version change, so after 17-3b it **must** carry (1) a ratified `xtask/abi-ratifications.toml` entry covering `maos:spirit@2.1.0` — `wit_corpus.rs`'s version-binding test reds without it — and (2) the re-issue of all 4 provenance-pinned components with the required `[toolchain]` table (`check-equiv-fixture-provenance`); `wit_corpus.rs` counts move again (R17-66). The frames are lossless from 17-3b on: AC4's consent vector can rely on `consent-envelope` reaching the guest.

*⚠ R13 (2026-10-17, 17-3d creation at `39cd7966`; ruled 2026-10-18):* the ACs below are re-read through §R13 (R17-141…R17-156). The binding contract is `implementation-artifacts/17-3d-wasm-log-recall.md`: AC1's foreign frame is the `--once` operator seed (pid 0), and its exact rows are fixed as an ordered subsequence (R17-141); recall is incarnation-scoped at origin (R17-143) with SQL-correct pages (R17-144); the grant is a new `[capabilities.required.log]` on schema 5 with `LogRecall`/`LogFetch` minted per call (R17-145, R17-146); omission proofs assert `unavailable`, not SIGSYS (R17-147); kernel-Δ measured +18 `src_lines` / +13 tokei (R17-151). AC4 has no positive vector under emitter scope; **ruled FW-07 A**: 17-3d delivers non-Rust emitter-scoped recall parity, exit line 2b and AC4's safety half, and FR29's participant/consent completion is owned by `17-8-participant-recall-and-consent-envelopes` (R17-148, R17-156).

*⚠ R14 (2026-10-18, 17-3d preflight; operator rulings D-17-3d-PF-R1…R4):* AC3's transport is superseded — recall rides the runner's ADR-032 stdio as CBOR-tagged records during an active export, mediated by maos-bin's session loop: no socketpair, no inherited descriptor, no `sendto`/`recvfrom`, no Yama condition (R17-157; ADR-060 `:230-268`). AC3's kernel lines become the manifest literal and the deletion of the dormant scheduler recall seam, with no raise (R17-158). Recall discloses only bus frames and MCP invocation records, never audit, receipt or identity rows (R17-159); replies fit the codec's budgets, with `reply-too-large` when they cannot (R17-160). Recalling an earlier incarnation is a non-goal (R17-163).

*Closes · Δ:* FR29 **form parity** for the non-Rust form (`prd/functional-requirements.md:75`): emitter-scoped, incarnation-bound, mediated recall; FR29's participant and consent clauses → `17-8` (R17-156) · epic exit line 2b · kernel-Δ **FLAG-Winston, no raise** (R17-157, R17-158): the `CapabilitiesRequired` literal (+1, measured) and the deletion-only removal of the dormant recall seam; the 2026-09-26 descriptor lines (`sendto`, `recvfrom`, the `SandboxSpec` field and its CLOEXEC clear) are withdrawn · maos-iac (scope, disclosure and pages at origin) · maos-bin (mediation in the session loop) · maos-wasm-host (the host import). After 17-3c. Downstream: only 20-7 AC4's recall leg waits on it (R17-154); 17-8 and 17-9 follow it.

- **AC1 (command) — epic exit line 2b.** The TS Spirit recalls its own frames, and fetching another Spirit's frame id returns `recall-error::scope-violation` — a filter cannot name a pid (R17-23; `crates/maos-domain/src/log_recall.rs:14-38`; `ScopeViolation` raised at `crates/maos-iac/src/adapter/log_recall.rs:482-487`). Both are observed through the command line; the exact rows are fixed at creation.
- **AC2 — the contract grows additively.** `maos:spirit@2.1.0` adds the `recall` import; hosts ship first, and `@2.0` guests keep running on `@2.1` hosts (the Q5 matrix, R17-43). The frame-kind label is an open `string`, so a new `FrameKindLabel` never breaks a deployed guest (R17-43).
- **AC3 — transport and identity (ADR-060 §Amendment).** A connected socketpair per spawn: the runner's end inherited, the service's end kept by maos-bin. The service binds the scheduler `spirit_pid` at spawn and never takes a pid from the guest; it checks `Scope::LogRecall` against the Spirit's grant (`cap_policy/mod.rs:185-188`), since the adapter checks none. The Yama `ptrace_scope ≥ 1` condition is stated where deployment is documented. `sendto` and `recvfrom` join the T2 allow-list with their leave-one-out proofs.
- **AC4 — FR29's consent clause.** Recall honors A2A consent envelopes (FR29 — *"kernel scopes results to participant frames and honors A2A consent envelopes"*), which needs 17-3b's lossless frames: a vector in which a consent-gated frame is withheld from a WASM Spirit.
- **AC5 — no unbacked latency claim.** The story states no recall latency number; `20-7-stable-runner-perf-gates` measures it against its NFR-Perf-5 fixture (R17-42).

*Why it is a story and not a tracker row.* This lane slipped three times and each slip had a different cause: **E14-A3** slipped invisibly (Epic 14's retro actions were never written to `sprint-status.yaml` at all, so the W0 → "W1+W2" move had no reader); **E15-A2** resolved the ambiguity — "W1+W2" was never a schedule — and fixed the wave at W1, parallel to Epic 16, on the measured ground that the lane touches no `crates/maos-bin/src/main.rs` line and therefore cannot contend for the write lock; **E16-A4** then found it still at `backlog` after Epic 16 shipped seven stories. ### 17-6-t2-sandbox-repair-and-proven-red — T2 sandbox repair: a T2 child starts, a forbidden syscall dies, proven red in CI ◆ FLAG-Winston

*Closes · Δ:* R17-25 (T2's Landlock ruleset denies every `execve`), R17-26 (a Rust binary dies before `main` under the T2 allow-list), R17-27 for the three T2 suites (each passes by skipping), R17-24's missing launch line (AC5) and R17-37 (AC6 — nine in-process manifests declare a tier they never get) · kernel-Δ **+97 tokei / +112 `src_lines` across 4 files** — dev pass +80/+95, operator-ratified review inode guard +17/+17; the ruled +71/+83 (lean prototype, E16-A7, RT-3) plus the arm-measured aarch64 `ppoll` row (RT-6) and the operator-approved prctl(PR_GET_AUXV)-only delta (T8 finding); the ELF exec-set resolver is the leaf crate `maos-exec-deps`; re-pin `kernel-core-baseline.toml` and draw the `maos-kernel-core` kloc grant in the same commit · outside the kernel: the three suites, the `wasm-host-tests` job, and AC6's admission/SDK refusal, nine manifest lines and two published pages (en + ko)

*Why a story of its own (operator decision 2026-09-25, 17-3a RT-8).* T2 is a sandbox tier that has never started a process: no shipped path calls `spawn_sandboxed` (R17-24), and every proof of it in the repo passes by skipping (R17-27). Folded into 17-2 it would queue behind 17-1's egress proxy and shrink to a cgroup sub-AC; folded into 17-3b it would falsify that story's *kernel-Δ 0*. **Sequence:** after 17-3a, which prices every line — this story may not leave `backlog` until 17-3a Q4b has a MEASURED verdict — and before 17-2 (AC1 needs a live T2 fixture child) and **17-3c** (its runner runs under T2); 17-3b runs in parallel (R17-51).

- **AC1 (command) — epic exit line 3.** In CI, `cargo test --locked -p maos-wasm-host --test t2_sandbox_kill -- --nocapture` shows `forbidden_syscall_killed_by_t2_with_sigsys` (signal 31 on `ptrace`, classified `SandboxViolation { T2 }`), `benign_process_survives_t2_under_same_spec` (`/bin/true`) and `benign_rust_probe_survives_t2_under_same_spec` (a Rust binary reaches `main` and starts a thread) exiting 0, and the ungranted `cat` refused with `Permission denied` naming the file — zero `SKIP`, read from public annotations on all four images (`ubuntu-24.04`, `ubuntu-26.04`, `ubuntu-24.04-arm`, `ubuntu-26.04-arm`; D-17-6-G's x64-only fallback if arm cannot be measured).
- **AC2 — a T2 child can start.** `prepare_landlock` (`linux.rs:163-202`) grants `Execute` and `ReadFile` on the resolved program and on exactly what loads it — the dynamic loader (`Execute|ReadFile`) and the libraries (`ReadFile`), resolved parent-side by the leaf crate `maos-exec-deps` (dynamic — Q4b layer 1; RT-3) — never on `/`, never on a directory the resolver did not name; a resolver error refuses the spawn. Proven red in CI: without the grant the spawn fails `EACCES`.
- **AC3 — a Rust program reaches `main` under the allow-list.** The kill filter installs first (`vec![kill_bpf, bpf]` — the reverse never installed, R17-44(a)); the allow-list gains exactly `poll` (x86_64 row), `rt_sigaction`, `clone3` and, on aarch64, the arm-measured `ppoll`, plus `prctl` restricted to `PR_GET_AUXV` (operator-approved 2026-09-28 — uutils `cat` on ubuntu-26.04) (`linux.rs`). Proven red in CI: the verbatim order reds the step; dropping `poll`/`ppoll` or `rt_sigaction` reds the Rust benign probe with signal 11 (x64; signal 5 on arm), dropping `clone3` with rc 101, while the forbidden probe still dies of `SIGSYS`.
- **AC4 — the three T2 suites cannot pass by skipping.** `t2_sandbox_kill.rs:32-41`, `sandbox_enforcement_linux.rs:19-28` and `maos-escape-detector/tests/common/mod.rs:152-175` stop turning a refused spawn into `ok` in CI (ambient `CI`/`GITHUB_ACTIONS` declares the runner capable; off CI each helper keeps exactly its skip set and `SKIP` text); `wasm-host-tests` (`discipline.yml:2914-2928`) runs the T2 suite with `--nocapture`, and its *"self-skips without CAP_SYS_ADMIN"* comment (`:2912-2913`) goes. Proven red: a CI run whose spawn is refused reads red.
- **AC5 — T2 is a production path (operator decision 2026-09-25, 17-3a follow-up D2 = a).** `spawn_and_bridge` — the single launch primitive for every spawned form, native subprocess and WASM component alike (`crates/maos-host/src/lib.rs:7-20`) — gains the admitted `SandboxSpec` (`BridgeSpawnSpec.sandbox` — tier, scopes, caps, cgroup id; RT-2) and routes `SandboxTier::T2` specs through `spawn_sandboxed` **on Linux only** (refused typed elsewhere, V-5) (`security/sandbox/mod.rs:136`) instead of the bare `Command::new` (`runtime.rs:465`), keeping the ADR-023 argv-prefix check and the piped stdio; a refused T2 spawn is a typed error, never a bare fallback. The primitive refuses, typed (`BridgeError::SandboxRefused`), a tier no spawn path applies (`T1` — no UID mechanism; `T4` — the reserved WASM tool sandbox); `T0`/`T3` stay a plain spawn (T3 applied by 17-1 AC4). The cli_wrapper admission floor is **exactly T3** (ADR-060 clause 4; RT-1): a `T4` request is refused `ECliWrapperRequiresT3` before any probe. Proven red (`maos-kernel-core/tests/bridge_t2_route_17_6.rs`, four images): the probe through `spawn_and_bridge` reads T0 `Exited{0}`, **T2 `Signaled{31}`**, T3 `Exited{0}`, T1/T4 `SandboxRefused`; routing T2 bare turns the T2 row `Exited{0}`. The `maos run` SIGSYS leg moves to **17-3c** (R17-47, R17-51) — the first production T2 child.
- **AC6 — an in-process manifest claims no sandbox it does not get (operator decision 2026-09-25, re-ruled 2026-09-26 on the re-measurement, R17-37).** A `rust-inproc` Spirit runs inside the daemon with no process sandbox, so the only true `[sandbox] tier` for it is `T0` (*trusted local*, `crates/maos-domain/src/invariants/i9.rs:77`). Admission gate 6 (`crates/maos-bin/src/admission.rs`), with `class_section` bound at gate 4, refuses (`rust_inproc_requires_t0`; a `[sandbox]` without `tier` defaults to T2 and is refused too), typed, a `rust-inproc` manifest that declares any other tier, naming the tier and the form; the doc comment at `:196-203` (*a manifest cannot select its own tier, so it cannot be refused for one*) is rewritten to match AC5 and AC6. The SDK self-check (`crates/maos-spirit-sdk/src/spirit_test/manifest.rs:130-139`) refuses the same pair, so `spirit test` fails first. The nine first-party declarations become `tier = "T0"` — `spirits/{architect,orchestrator,reviewer}/manifest.toml:27`, `spirits/butler/manifest.toml:72`, `spirits/digest/manifest.toml:29`, `spirits/mira/manifest.toml:54`, `spirits/nash/manifest.toml:50`, `spirits/observer/manifest.toml:67` and `spirits/researcher/manifest.toml:99`, with the two comments that justify `T2` (`observer/manifest.toml:65-66`, `researcher/manifest.toml:97-98`) rewritten. The section stays required (gate 6's `required("sandbox")` at `:462`, the SDK's `ManifestMinimalShape.sandbox` at `manifest.rs:54`), so no schema, SDK-API or template change; no effective tier changes either, because admission discards the manifest tier and admits at `DEFAULT_FLOOR` (16-6 §11 row 7). The two published pages that teach tier names the parser refuses — `docs-site/docs/cookbook/hello-world-spirit.md:38-39` (`baseline`) and `docs-site/docs/cookbook/manifest-fields.md:34-35` (`baseline | hardened | paranoid`), ko twins `:39-40` and `:35-36` — name `T0`–`T4` and say an in-process Spirit declares `T0`, English and Korean together (`check-ko-coverage`); `cli-wrapper-spirit.md` stays 17-1 AC5's. Inline test manifests that pair `rust-inproc` with another tier migrate in the same commit (1 inline + the 8 `spirit_smoke` pins). The cookbook blocks' `[sandbox]` and `[resources]` must PARSE (`crates/maos-manifest/tests/cookbook_sections_17_6.rs`, RT-8); the rest of the blocks → 17-3b (R17-49). Proven red: a `rust-inproc` fixture declaring `T2` is refused by admission and by `manifest_self_check`, while the nine relabelled manifests and the four that already declare `T0` (`spirits/hello-spirit`, `crates/maos-spirit-hello`, `templates/spirit-rust`, `examples/example-spirit`) still load.

### 17-7-t3-worker-retained-lifecycle-and-terminal-facts — T3 Worker retained lifecycle and terminal facts ◆ FLAG-Winston

*Created 2026-10-11 by operator decision (17-2 preflight, D-17-2-FW-07; R17-113).*

*⚠ R12 (2026-10-13, 17-7 creation, HEAD `37c34be4`; story `implementation-artifacts/17-7-t3-worker-retained-lifecycle-and-terminal-facts.md`, decision record `17-7-flag-winston-decision.md`, ratified 2026-10-13):* the ACs below stand except where §R12 names the words it supersedes — R17-117 (AC1's three-control distinction is met by variant A, ruled; runtime-only evidence is the per-spawn fallback), R17-118 (AC2's startup identity is `create` → `init` → pidfile → `start --attach`), R17-120 (stale cites: the guard drop is `runtime.rs:948`, not `:503-510`; `FaultCause` is `supervision.rs:92-114`), R17-123 (AC4's failing-`on_unload` case needs a post-unload stop independent of the hook), R17-124 (AC4's lease end), R17-126 (AC5's inventory) and R17-130 (the *Kernel-Δ* paragraph's unmeasured +120…+360 is replaced by the measured prototype figures; the ruled ceiling is variant A's ≤ 25574 / ≤ 19946, exact at landing). *Other crates* also gains maos-audit (R17-128) and, if a revoke-only seam is needed, maos-egress. The exact AC1 rows are fixed in the story.

*Closes · Δ:*

- **Closes:** `deferred-work.md:1119` (authoritative T3 OOM/signal facts) and `:1120` (a short-lived T3 Worker is refused as a failed launch).
- **Kernel-Δ:** FLAG-Winston, **measured by a prototype at creation (E16-A7)**. The story may not leave `backlog` without that measurement. The scout estimate is +120…+360 physical lines across `t3/{argv,spawn,child}.rs` and `cli_wrapper/runtime.rs`; this is [INFERENCE], unmeasured, and not a ledger forecast.
- **Other crates:** maos-bin (`supervision.rs` classifiers; `worker_spawn.rs` start/report/watch) and maos-domain (`SandboxInspectReport` startup identity; a typed terminal fact beside `FaultCause::OomKilled`, `maos-domain/src/supervision.rs:63-85`).
- **Order:** after 17-2.

*Why a story of its own (R17-113).* The two rows share an evidence-lifetime obstacle, but not one root cause:

- **Retention alone is not enough.** `--rm` (`t3/argv.rs:77-106`) destroys post-exit inspect evidence. Yet an exited container reports `State.Pid = 0`, conmon maps signal death to 128+signal, and its OOM marker fires on `oom` **or** `oom_kill`. Removing `--rm` therefore fixes neither row.
- **The supervised child is the client.** MAOS supervises the foreground Podman client (`t3/child.rs:10-25`). Both the pidfd observer and bridge finalization classify the client's status (`supervision.rs:731-745,1813-1838`; `runtime.rs:917-979`). The container guard is dropped before journaling (`runtime.rs:503-510`).
- **It is a different size class.** The fix is a lifecycle cutover with its own charter, not a 17-2 resource-flag change.

- **AC1 (command).** In the `epic-17-exit` T3 lane, on both x64 images:
  - A Worker whose task exits immediately completes and is journaled as a completion, not a failed launch.
  - Three bounded controls stay distinguishable: an intentional `exit(137)`, an external SIGKILL, and a `memory.max` OOM under 17-2's `[resources].memory_max_mb`. They are not all journaled `fault.non-zero-exit`.
  - Where the only evidence is 128+signal, the signal is reported **unknown**, never inferred.
  - The exact rows are fixed at creation.
- **AC2 — runtime-owned startup identity.**
  - An owned `--pidfile` in a private per-spawn location replaces `inspect_container_host_pid`'s polling (`t3/spawn.rs:229-258`). Stale or missing files are refused, never read as success.
  - `SandboxInspectReport.pid` (`maos-domain/src/sandbox.rs:297-305`) distinguishes a live PID from a historical one. A historical PID is never a kill target.
- **AC3 — terminal facts before classification.**
  - One typed terminal snapshot is read by owned CID while the container is retained: state, `ExitCode`, `OOMKilled`, error and times.
  - The snapshot reaches **both** `BindingCore::on_child_exit` and bridge finalization before `classify_exit`, so a late extra row cannot repair a written `task.orphaned`.
  - Preserve the ECHILD single-classification rule and the PlannedStop latch (`supervision.rs:370-382`).
  - The runtime-client status stays separate from the container status. `OOMKilled` is bounded evidence under an explicitly tested authority contract, not proof of init's death cause.
- **AC4 — ownership and cleanup.**
  - The CID is owned before start. Cleanup is by owned CID, never by the requested name (`t3/spawn.rs:190-205`, `t3/child.rs:88-96`; R17-114).
  - Explicit bounded removal, including anonymous volumes, replaces `--rm`.
  - The unbounded named-kill `.status()` calls (`runtime.rs:481-496`, `supervision.rs:914-919`) become bounded, and residue is reported rather than claimed removed.
  - The proxy lease ends at confirmed payload termination (`worker_spawn.rs:770-798`; `egress.rs:160-185`).
  - A stale container after a daemon crash is never restarted with its stored bearer environment.
  - A regression in which `WorkerSpirit::on_unload` (`supervision.rs:1979-1987`) panics or exceeds its hook budget still leaves no running container and no unreported residue (`deferred-work.md:1126`; routed from the closed 17-1 row `:992` on 2026-10-11).
- **AC5 — no workaround survives.**
  - Migrate the client-argv helpers (`worker_supervision_16_3.rs:1819-1853`; `root_shutdown_unload_16_3.rs:285-324`).
  - Delete the three-second T3 holds in the fixtures (`spirits/worker/src/bin/worker-cli-fixture.rs:23-24,41-45`; `worker-probe-fixture.rs:80-95`).
  - Rewrite the cookbook limitation sentences (EN `cli-wrapper-spirit.md:71`, KO `:72`) to describe shipped behavior.

### 17-8-participant-recall-and-consent-envelopes — Participant recall and consent envelopes for both forms

*Created 2026-10-18 by operator ruling D-17-3d-FW-07 option A (17-3d creation; R17-148, R17-156). After 17-3d. No consumer in Epics 18–21 waits on it. The ACs below are the routed obligation; story creation re-measures them at its own HEAD (rule 9). ⚠ R14 (2026-10-18, 17-3d preflight): AC1 is the command and re-states exit line 2b (R17-162), AC2 inherits 17-3d's disclosure allow-list (R17-159), and AC5 is a delivered obligation, not a decision (R17-164).*

*⚠ R17 (2026-10-20, 17-8 creation at `1241ce52`; story `implementation-artifacts/17-8-participant-recall-and-consent-envelopes.md`):* the ACs below are re-measured in **§R17 (R17-183…R17-198)** and tightened in the story, whose ACs supersede these where they differ. Read first: the transaction premise (R17-183), the resolver and role facts (R17-184, R17-185), the absent production gate (R17-186), the absent rupture transition (R17-187, D-17-8-03), the header-only representation (R17-188, D-17-8-01), the expiry encoding (R17-189), and the 17-3d vectors this story migrates (R17-194).

*Closes · Δ:* FR29's participant and consent clauses (`prd/functional-requirements.md:75`; ADR-013 `docs/adr/ADR-013-log-recall-kernel-mediated-spirit-abi-capability.md:11`) for **both** forms · epic exit line 2b re-stated · kernel-Δ expected 0 (the recall adapter and the TL live in `maos-iac`), measured at creation · maos-iac (TL side tables, participant predicate, consent evaluation), maos-bin (17-3d's mediation path and receipts) · the guest surface is already in place (`recall-error::consent-withheld` ships in 17-3d's `maos:spirit@2.1.0`).

- **AC1 (command) — participant recall end to end.** One integration suite, named at creation (e.g. `crates/maos-bin/tests/participant_recall_17_8.rs`, run like 17-3d's `recall_17_3d` in the delegated envelope with exact pass-count guards), drives native (the Researcher port) and WASM (17-3d's mediated path) through: frames the caller sent; frames addressed to it by id; by role; one of several recipients; written through the direct and the DRR paths; a valid, an expired and a ruptured envelope; a header-only envelope; a remote artifact without side tables. Every vector asserts the exact token-bearing rows and refusal receipts and zero unauthorized payload bytes. **Exit line 2b is re-stated:** under participant scope the `--once` seed is a received frame, so its `fetch` succeeds (R2 token-bearing, no R3); 17-8 fixes the new exact rows and their proven-red, and replaces 17-3d's AC4.1 safety vector with these consent vectors (R17-162).
- **AC2 — participant scope.** `recall`/`fetch` return frames the caller sent **or received** (addressed by id or role), incarnation-bound as 17-3d made them. The TL persists every recipient — today only the first is written (`crates/maos-iac/src/adapter.rs:584-595`) — in a side table written in the same transaction as the frame row on both the direct and DRR paths (`adapter.rs:496-499`, `adapter/drr_scheduler.rs:291-311`; side-table precedent `transparency_log.rs:312-319`). Recipient identity binds the admitted Spirit of the incarnation, never a guest-supplied name. Native and WASM share one predicate. 17-3d's raw-kind disclosure allow-list applies unchanged (R17-159): participant scope widens *whose* frames, never *which kinds*.
- **AC3 — persisted consent envelopes.** The frame's `ConsentEnvelope` (`crates/maos-domain/src/frame.rs:431-444`) is persisted with the row and evaluated at disclosure: expired (`now_ns > valid_until_ns`, the router's semantics `crates/maos-a2a-core/src/router.rs:1401-1453`) or ruptured since delivery → omitted from `recall`; `fetch` returns `recall-error::consent-withheld` and writes `log.fetch.refused:consent-withheld`. 17-3d's pointer at the old consent comment (`log_recall.rs:489-497` at `39cd7966`) is replaced by the evaluation.
- **AC4 — FR29's positive vector.** A frame delivered under a valid envelope is recalled and fetched by its recipient; after expiry or rupture it is withheld — native and WASM, through 17-3d's mediation path, token-bearing rows.
- **AC5 — header-only elision is delivered.** ADR-013's header-only consent (`docs/adr/ADR-013-log-recall-kernel-mediated-spirit-abi-capability.md:11`) is an obligation of this story (D-17-3d-FW-07 A): a header-only consent frame returns its participant-visible header through `recall` and never its payload through `fetch`, for both forms. `ConsentEnvelope` has no such field (`crates/maos-domain/src/frame.rs:431-444`); creation measures and ratifies the frame and WIT change it needs. Dropping the obligation needs an explicit superseding operator ruling and an ADR-013 amendment; FR29 stays PARTIAL until then (R17-164).
- **AC6 — readers stay honest.** Independent TL readers (`maos-audit` query/export, `crates/maos-bin/src/cross_wall_log_read.rs`, SIEM, the `check_rto`/`check_migration_merkle` fixtures) stay compatible, proven on fixtures with and without the side tables; a remote artifact without them degrades to emitter scope with a typed marker and zero payload disclosure.

### 17-9-capability-issuance-quota-window — Capability issuance quota window ◆ FLAG-Winston

*Created 2026-10-18 by operator ruling D-17-3d-PF-R3 (17-3d preflight; R17-161). After 17-8 (sequencing only). The ACs below are the routed obligation; story creation re-measures them at its own HEAD (rule 9) and measures the kernel figure by prototype (E16-A7).*

*⚠ R18 (2026-10-22, 17-9 creation at `3a7c4260`; story `implementation-artifacts/17-9-capability-issuance-quota-window.md`, decision record `17-9-flag-winston-decision.md`, ruled the same day):* the ACs below are re-measured in **§R18 (R17-199…R17-214)** and tightened in the story, whose ACs supersede these where they differ. Read first: the exclusive boundary (R17-199), the window that 1b.2 already fixed (R17-200), the signing-failure leak (R17-201), the events that never fired (R17-202), the FR4 doorbell (R17-203), ADR-016's different unit (R17-204, now `18-5`), the tokenless fallbacks (R17-208), the accounting algorithm (R17-211) and the per-incarnation scope (R17-212). Kernel-Δ measured **+57 / +56**; grant ruled ≤ 26361 / ≤ 20594, exact at landing.

*Closes · Δ:* Story 1b.2's per-window quota semantics (`epic-1b-evaluator-path-audit-spine-capability-mediation-baseline-v01.md:92-96`: `ContextPressure` at 80 %, `ContextLimit` at 95 %, `EContextExhausted` above 100 %) and `deferred-work.md:1076` (issuance charged before scope mapping and policy, never refunded) · kernel-Δ **FLAG-Winston**, measured at creation: `capability/mod.rs` (`issue_with_mediation`; the literal budget 1,000,000 at `:189-190`) and the `maos-capability` quota (`crates/maos-capability/src/cap_quota/mod.rs:63-123`; `reset_window` has no production caller) · maos-bin only if a budget source is wired there.

*Why a story of its own (R17-161).* Every mint charges a per-pid counter that never resets in production, so any long-lived Spirit eventually loses every capability: 17-3c's `iac.send` mints per emitted frame and 17-3d's `log.recall`/`log.fetch` per call. 17-3d keeps its own issuance linear; the window is kernel capability accounting outside its charter.

- **AC1 (command) — a long-lived Spirit keeps its capabilities.** A test named at creation (e.g. `cargo test --locked -p maos-kernel-core --test issuance_quota_window_17_9`) drives one pid through more than one budget's worth of issuances across windows: every issuance within a window's budget succeeds, the first over-budget issuance in a window is `EContextExhausted`, and the next window succeeds again.
- **AC2 — a defined window.** The quota resets on a window chosen and measured at creation (time-based or turn-based), from one named budget source instead of the literal at `capability/mod.rs:189`; the choice is ratified with the charter.
- **AC3 — charged for issued tokens only.** Scope mapping and policy run before the charge, or a denied or approval-required request is refunded (`deferred-work.md:1076`); a policy denial never consumes quota.
- **AC4 — pressure events per window.** `ContextPressure` (80 %) and `ContextLimit` (95 %) fire once per window (`cap_quota/mod.rs:96-116` fire once per lifetime today).
- **AC5 — typed exhaustion everywhere.** The `iac.send`, `log.recall`/`log.fetch` and MCP minting paths surface exhaustion as their typed denial with its receipt (17-3d: `capability-denied`), never a tokenless fallback.
