*Created 2026-10-22 by operator ruling D-17-9-PF-R4b A at 17-9's preflight (`deferred-work.md:1072`; R17-222), under the operator's decision rule. No ordering edge inside Epic 17. The ACs below are the routed obligation; story creation re-measures them at its own HEAD (rule 9).*

*⚠ 17-10 creation (2026-10-23, HEAD `8e93ffad`; story `implementation-artifacts/17-10-live-sandbox-anomaly-consumer.md`, decisions D-17-10-FW-01…09 PROPOSED):* re-measured OLD→NEW, applied in place below — (1) the test caller is `spawned_scope_17_3c.rs:1668-1678` (call `:1673`), not `:1630`; (2) maos-bin measured **27614 = ceiling 27614** (tokei 14.0.0, `xtask/kloc.toml:517`), zero headroom — the request is measured **at landing** (contract 2026-10-23), not at creation. Further premise corrections (whole-log scan, the egress proxy's kind-8 refusals, the session root's immediate teardown, the fault-inject runner as the T2 child, the existing ABSENT wiring leg in `check-reza-production-path`) are in the story's §Current-tree corrections; the ACs below stand as tightened there.

*Closes · Δ:* NFR-Sec-3's production half — the kernel raises a structural alarm and its interpretation is operator- or Spirit-side (`prd/non-functional-requirements.md:36`) — and ADR-024's user-space correlation over a read-only Transparency-Log query/tail (`docs/adr/ADR-024-out-of-kernel-sandbox-escape-structural-detector.md:56-80`) · kernel-Δ 0 (ADR-024 selects query/tail, not a kernel broadcast) · maos-bin at zero headroom (27614 / 27614 measured at `8e93ffad`; exact request measured at landing).

*Why a story of its own.* `report_escape_anomalies` (`crates/maos-bin/src/escape_detector_consumer.rs:28`) has no daemon caller — its own doc says so (`:13-19`); the only callers are its unit test and `crates/maos-bin/tests/spawned_scope_17_3c.rs:1673`. A real SIGSYS is persisted as a SandboxBlock row at T2 finalization (`crates/maos-kernel-core/src/lifecycle/cli_wrapper/runtime.rs:1024-1033`) and no running daemon ever surfaces it.

- **AC1 (command) — a running daemon surfaces a real escape attempt.** A T2 child that issues a forbidden syscall yields its SandboxBlock row and, within a bound measured at creation, exactly one correlated anomaly on the operator surface named at creation; a planned stop yields none.
- **AC2 — read-only and failure-visible.** The consumer only reads the TL (query/tail per ADR-024) and never writes kernel state; a scan failure is typed and observable, never a silent "no anomaly".
- **AC3 — proven red in CI.** Disconnecting the consumer reds AC1 on a T2-capable runner (rule 11; the 17-6 images).

### 17-11-scope-selector-entitlement — Selector-level scope entitlement at issuance ◆ FLAG-Winston

*Created 2026-10-22 by operator ruling D-17-9-PF-R4d A at 17-9's preflight (`deferred-work.md:1074`, widened; R17-223), under the operator's decision rule. Before `18-1-butler-inference-seam-and-conformant-mcp`. The ACs below are the routed obligation; story creation re-measures them at its own HEAD (rule 9) and measures the kernel figure by prototype (E16-A7).*

*⚠ Creation 2026-10-23 at `8e93ffad` (story `implementation-artifacts/17-11-scope-selector-entitlement.md`, decision record `17-11-flag-winston-decision.md`, decisions D-17-11-FW-01…09 PROPOSED; evidence `17-11-evidence/creation/`). OLD→NEW at HEAD:* (1) the discriminant match is `cap_policy/mod.rs:159-167` (comment `:159-160`, `.any(…discriminant…)` `:161-164`, deny `:165-167`), not `:161-166`; (2) `Scope` spans `i1.rs:60-131` (selector-bearing variants `:62-111`), not `:60-90`; (3) kernel-Δ **measured 0 physical / 0 tokei** by an in-place prototype of the recommended home (`Scope::entitles` in `maos-domain`, kernel call site only; one content digest moves) — `maos-domain` +23 tokei; the in-kernel alternative measures +29 / +21; (4) **admission grants only `ProviderInfer`, `McpCall`, `IacSend`, `Loom*`, `Log*`** (`crates/maos-manifest/src/manifest.rs:820-867`) — no manifest field produces `FsRead`/`FsWrite`/`NetHttps`/`ProcExec`/`SubSpiritSpawn`/`Mem*`/`CliSubprocessSpawn`/`GatewaySend` grants, and `GatewaySend` is unmediated (`capability/mod.rs:105-108`); (5) AC3's regressions are real and executed: 7 `maos-bin` tests red on the prototype, all `ProviderInfer { ollama }` requested by the composition root (router default, `crates/maos-bin/src/main.rs:3571-3589`) against a declared `anthropic`; no manifest anywhere declares a `NetHttps` wildcard.

*Closes · Δ:* FR5's *"Spirit cannot exfiltrate data outside its declared capability scope"* (`prd/functional-requirements.md:28`) and capability mediation's token scoping (`prd/domain-specific-requirements.md:46`) **at issuance** · kernel-Δ **FLAG-Winston** (`capability/cap_policy/mod.rs`), measured at creation.

*Why a story of its own.* `PolicyTable::evaluate` admits a request when **any** declared manifest scope has the same enum discriminant (`crates/maos-kernel-core/src/capability/cap_policy/mod.rs:161-166`). `scope_to_intent` keeps the selector (`capability/mod.rs:86-89`), but nothing compares it, so every parameterized scope (`crates/maos-domain/src/invariants/i1.rs:60-90` — `FsRead`/`FsWrite` subtree, `NetHttps` domain, `ProcExec` binary, `SubSpiritSpawn` class, `ProviderInfer` provider, `IacSend` peer class, `MemRead`/`MemWrite` scope, `McpCall` server/tool, `CliSubprocessSpawn`, `GatewaySend`) is issued for undeclared selectors. Use-time checks compare a call to its token (e.g. MCP, `crates/maos-kernel-core/src/mcp/mod.rs:68-75`) and cannot repair an over-broad grant. 17-3c disclosed the MCP case only (`deferred-work.md:1074`).

- **AC1 (command) — only declared selectors are issued.** A test named at creation proves, for every parameterized scope, that the declared selector is issued and an undeclared one is `PolicyDenied` — before any quota charge (17-9's policy → quota → tokens order).
- **AC2 — selector semantics stated per scope**, measured and ratified at creation: exact match for identifiers (MCP server/tool, IAC peer class, gateway, provider, binary, sub-Spirit class, memory scope, CLI path and argv hash); normalized subtree containment with no `..` escape for `FsRead`/`FsWrite`; the `NetHttps` domain rule (exact or suffix) chosen and stated.
- **AC3 — no silent regression.** Every production and fixture manifest still receives what it declares; any caller found relying on an undeclared selector is fixed at origin or its manifest corrected, each named in the Debug Log.
- **AC4 — proven red in CI.** Restoring discriminant-only matching reds AC1 (rule 11).

So a Worker CLI that leaves a grandchild on inherited stdio (e.g. a spawned MCP/LSP server) pins **one pipe pair + two `cli-bridge-*` reader threads + one blocking-pool thread** for the grandchild's lifetime. NOT affected: crash detection (exit observer, 100/100 within 2 s) and daemon shutdown (bounded by `await_idle(grace)`, `supervision.rs:1240`; exit 1 naming the Worker, `:45-47`). ⚠ History: the Epic-16 retrospective first filed this as the cause of `nfr-rel-1`'s 6-hour hang and requested FLAG-Winston; that was WITHDRAWN — the hang was a harness race (`deferred-work.md`, corpus entry). **Precondition before any design (rule: measured, not read):** run the real Worker CLIs (`claude`, `codex`, per `worker_cli.rs`) through `maos run` and count surviving grandchildren holding stdio and their lifetimes; and re-measure under 17-1's `spawn_t3`, where the bridge's direct child becomes `podman run` and a container kill takes its grandchildren with it — the defect may dissolve for T3 Workers. **Owner: Winston.** If a bounded post-exit drain is chosen, it rides **17-1's** existing FLAG-Winston and kernel-Δ (`runtime.rs` is already on 17-1's Δ line) — no separate grant. |

**Exit-block verdict (rule 1, re-run 2026-09-20):** `cargo run -p xtask -- check-exit-commands` **PASSES** — 6 epics, 6 blocks, 33 tokens resolved, 4 owed, and **none of the four is Epic 17's**. Heads `maos` (`MAOS_BINARIES` `:152`), `cargo` (`DECLARED_NON_COMMANDS` `:139`) and `target/debug/maos` (stems at `:1085-1088`) all resolve; verbs `run` and `audit` are live `VERBS` rows (`verbs.rs:96`, `:108`). Epic 17's block therefore needs **no** AC1-created verb. ⚠ Caveat in the gate's own source (`:1580-1587`): a binary absent from `target/debug` is treated as absent and its tokens must then be owed by a named open story — `maos-wasm-runner` is currently warned as unbuilt, so 17-3b AC1's build step is what keeps line 2 honest.

## §R5 — the 17-3a spike (part A: premises disproved at creation, 2026-09-25, HEAD `92911f59`)

Filed under rule 9 by the 17-3a preflight round-table. Every row comes from `implementation-artifacts/17-3a-wasm-recall-and-componentize-spike.md` — §Premise corrections (P-numbers) and §Preflight round-table (RT-numbers). Rows marked **measured** were executed; the rest were read from the tree. Part B (the spike's verdicts) is appended below this table by 17-3a's dev pass, in its landing commit.

| # | Finding | Owner (rule 10) |
|---|---|---|
| **R17-23** | **17-3b AC1 and exit line 2 promise a refusal the type cannot express** (P7). The text says *"a filter naming another `spirit_pid` returns the typed refusal"*, but `LogRecallFilter` (`crates/maos-domain/src/log_recall.rs:13-38`) has no pid or team field — a security property pinned by `crates/maos-bin/tests/cross_team_consent_13_3.rs:290-308`. Scope is the caller's positional pid (`crates/maos-iac/src/adapter/log_recall.rs:309,315-316`). NEW: the channel binds the caller pid per spawn; the guest never supplies it. `recall` returns only the caller's frames. `fetch` of another Spirit's frame id → `LogRecallError::ScopeViolation` (`log_recall.rs:341-345`, raised at adapter `:482-487`), mapped to a WIT `recall-error`. A hostile same-uid connector gets nothing (17-3a Q4c). | `17-3b` (AC1 + exit-line-2 comment) |
| **R17-24** | **17-3b AC1's *"launched through `spawn_and_bridge` under the existing T2 path; exercised by `t2_sandbox_kill.rs:29-39`"* is false three ways** (P9). `spawn_and_bridge` spawns bare (`Command::new(&spec.program)`, `crates/maos-kernel-core/src/lifecycle/cli_wrapper/runtime.rs:465`). `spawn_sandboxed` (`security/sandbox/mod.rs:136`) has **zero production callers**. `t2_sandbox_kill.rs` never launches the runner. D-C's *kernel-Δ 0 by construction* covers the recall **transport**, not the runner's **sandbox**: putting the runner under T2 needs a line where the `Command` is built, which is kernel code. | `17-3b`; priced by 17-3a Q4b — ⚠ with D2 = a (2026-09-25) that launch line is 17-6 AC5's, so 17-3b's runner reaches T2 through the production path without a kernel line of its own; only a transport the spike proves must be an inherited fd still costs 17-3b kernel lines |
| **R17-25** | **T2 cannot start ANY program wherever Landlock is enforced — measured** (P21). `prepare_landlock` handles every V1 right (`security/sandbox/linux.rs:172`, including `Execute` and `ReadFile`) but only ever adds `ReadFile\|ReadDir` / `+WriteFile` rules (`:181-192`) — never `Execute`. With the locked `landlock 0.4.4` and the ruleset copied verbatim: no Landlock → exit 0; `t2_spec`'s empty scopes → `EACCES`; + an `FsRead` grant → `EACCES`; + `Execute` on `/` (positive control) → exit 0. **17-2 AC1's *T2 fixture child* therefore cannot exist**, and 17-3b's S2-for-WASM needs kernel lines. No shipped path depends on T2 (zero production callers), so this is a correctness gap, not a live regression. **Vehicle named by the operator on 2026-09-25: a new ◆ FLAG-Winston story, `17-6-t2-sandbox-repair-and-proven-red`, after 17-3a and before 17-2 and 17-3b** (the room's recommendation; folding it into 17-2 or 17-3b was declined). | **`17-6`** (AC2) — operator decision, 17-3a RT-8 |
| **R17-26** | **Under T2's seccomp allowlist a Rust binary dies before `main` — measured by emulation** (P19). `poll`, `ppoll` and `rt_sigaction` are absent from the allowlist (`linux.rs:214-267`, legacy `:26-28`; a mismatch returns `EPERM`, `:306-311`). Rust std's `init` aborts on `EPERM` from `poll` and asserts on `signal(SIGPIPE)`. A shim returning `EPERM` for either kills `hello`, `forbidden-syscall-probe` and `maos-wasm-runner` before `main` (`rc=134`; controls exit 0). 17-3a Q4b layers 2–3 price the minimal additions plus runner-side mitigations **in CI**; every surviving line is a kernel line in the 98-file pin. | **`17-6`** (AC3) |
| **R17-27** | **Every T2 proof in the repo passes by skipping** (P20). `t2_sandbox_kill.rs:32-41` and `sandbox_enforcement_linux.rs:19-23` return early on `PermissionDenied`; `maos-escape-detector/tests/common/mod.rs:152-166` does so on `PermissionDenied` or `Unsupported`. Locally: `seccomp apply failed` ×3, then `3 passed`. Filed under rule 9 in the spike's landing commit. A non-author re-executed the Q1, Q3, Q4 and Q5 probes (`evidence/a6-reexec-orchestrator.log`). Text marked [INFERENCE] was read, not run.

| # | Measured outcome | OLD → NEW | Owner (rule 10) |

The release HEAD runner runs `on-start` and one real framed `handle-frame`, exits 0, and emits a decodable frame. The debug runner compiles the same component in 10,506.735 ms, over `COMPILE_TIMEOUT` = 10 s (`crates/maos-wasm-host/src/runner.rs:140`), and exits 3 (*compile-bomb*); release takes 792.820 ms. A clean Node 20 install cannot load `@napi-rs/lzma-linux-x64-gnu` (`@napi-rs/lzma@1.5.1` needs Node ^22.20). StarlingMonkey is embedded: 12,259,559 B. | OLD (17-3b AC1, exit line 2, `:98`, `:20`): a componentize-js build, then `cargo build … && target/debug/maos run …`. NEW: Node 22 in the job that componentizes (`example-spirit-ts-tests`, `discipline.yml:1086-1088`: 20 → 22). The direct `componentize-js` 0.23.0 CLI (jco 1.35.0 nests 0.22.0). `cargo build --release …` and `target/release/…` in exit line 2, or a precompiled `.cwasm` built with the runtime's exact engine config (R17-42). The component is a CI artifact, never committed. | **`17-3b`** |

(b) Guest `console.log` reaches neither stdout nor stderr: the WASI context inherits no stdio (`host_state.rs:20-25`). (c) `jco guest-types` declares `handleFrame(…): Array<IacFrame>` and never the thrown `halt`. (d) `wit/spirit.wit:216-218` says the runner *is sandboxed by the existing T2 path*, which is false (P9/P21) and the same class as `runner.rs:30-37` (RT-9). | NEW: map an instantiate-time fuel trap to exit 4; design a guest diagnostics channel, or document that `console.log` is discarded; state the thrown `halt` shape in the TS author path; correct the WIT comment together with `runner.rs:30-37`. | **`17-3b`** |

All 4 provenance `source_sha256` rows drift. `check-wasm-form-equiv` stays green. HEAD's runner compiles unchanged, so P4 is false: an unimplemented import surfaces only at instantiate, exit 3. The 4 pinned components rebuild at HEAD (rustc 1.98.1, wit-bindgen 0.44.0, wasm-tools 1.259.0) to **different** `wasm_sha256`s. The provenance file records no toolchain, so the cause cannot be measured, and no CI job rebuilds any component: the *scoped-nightly byte-rebuild* cited at `xtask/src/check_equiv_fixture_provenance.rs:15-17` does not exist. `abi-diff` (cargo-public-api 0.52.0) has no `LogRecall*`/`LogFetch` in scope. | OLD (AC2, `:99`; R17-28, R17-30): the 8 fixture guests re-built, plus an `abi-ratifications.toml` entry for the recall types. NEW: re-issue the **4** pinned components with the build toolchain recorded in `tests/fixtures/wasm/equiv-fixtures.provenance.toml` and a CI step that rebuilds and compares them (or the gate doc drops the phantom job); add the 5 corpus rows; drop the ratification clause, which is moot (measured). | **`17-3b`** |
| **R17-41** | **Q3 — `frame_bridge` can carry the four fields losslessly, but not additively.** A spike bridge round-trips a frame with intent, consent envelope, lineage and a typed `Scope` byte-identically: 1,025 B of canonical CBOR (nested `PriorDistillateRef.intent_lineage` and `DecisionDispatch.working_memory_digest_refs` still default in the lift, and the probe frame carried none — review qualifier). HEAD's bridge loses all four: 476 B, `readonly/none/empty-lineage/empty-scope`. Growing `iac-frame` from 8 to 11 fields makes every `@1.0` component fail type-check: `expected record of 11 fields, found 8 fields`. HEAD's bridge against that WIT fails at `frame_bridge.rs:216`, `:305` and `:404`. `Scope` is `#[non_exhaustive]`, and the spike refuses an unknown future variant rather than lowering it. | OLD (AC3, `:100`): rides AC2's *additive* `@1.1.0`. AC2 and AC3 may not share one additive claim. The `Scope` projection gets an explicit future-variant policy. | **`17-3b`** |
| **R17-42** | **Q4 (4a/4c/4d) — ADR-060 §D-C works as written with kernel-Δ 0, and the peer check is what makes it safe.** A recall guest's import reaches the real `LogRecallAdapter` over a daemon-chosen socket path. `recall` returns only A's frames, and `fetch` of B's frame returns `recall-error::scope-violation`. With the service's `SO_PEERCRED` check on (the runner's pid bound at spawn), a same-uid attacker is refused. With it off, the same-target control, the attacker reads A's frames. One-shot accept + unlink leaves nothing to connect to (ENOENT). The inherited-fd variant works too: a sibling can `O_PATH` `/proc/<runner>/fd/3`, but `open` → ENXIO and `pidfd_getfd` → EPERM under Yama `ptrace_scope=1` (a host policy). A precompiled `.cwasm` is engine-config-bound: mismatched flags → exit 3. Latency over 1,000 calls: recall p50 0.71–0.75 ms, p99 1.28–1.32 ms. Per-call cost climbs within one service lifetime from ~0.09 ms over the first 10 calls to ~1.2 ms over the last 10, for recall and fetch alike. `idx_tlog_spirit_pid` exists (`crates/maos-iac/src/adapter/transparency_log.rs:300-301`), and the cause is NOT-MEASURED. | OLD (AC1, `:98`; R17-23): *the channel binds the caller pid*. NEW: the service MUST verify the peer (`SO_PEERCRED` pid == the runner it spawned) and accept once, with the socket 0600 in a 0700 dir; without that check same-uid code reads another Spirit's frames (measured). Recall latency is measured against a realistically sized log (the NFR-Perf-5 fixture that `20-7` builds) before AC1 claims it. Whether the transport survives T2 is Q4b. | **`17-3b`** |
| **R17-43** | **Q5 — the measured compatibility matrix.** A `@1.0` fixture on a `@1.1` host → exit 0. A `@1.1` recall guest on the HEAD runner → exit 3: `imports instance maos:spirit/recall@1.1.0, but a matching implementation was not found`. A package-only bump links both ways (exit 0 and 0). **One** added `frame-kind-label` case fails both ways (exit 3 and 3). wasmtime 46.0.3 applies semver lookup only when both versions are the same API (`component/linker.rs:41-54`). | OLD (AC2): *additive*. NEW: hosts ship before guests. Any change to a recall type is a lock-step break, so a closed `frame-kind-label` (30 cases; `FrameKindLabel` is `#[non_exhaustive]`) breaks every deployed recall guest for each new label. Carry the label as an open `string`, or accept lock-step upgrades. The matrix above becomes 17-3b's compatibility check. | **`17-3b`** |
| **R17-44** | **Q4b — T2's seccomp layer has never installed anywhere; the measured repair is +25/−1 across 5 files, and under it the recall transport is the inherited fd.** (a) Order defect: `linux.rs:339` documents kill-first but `:346` returns `vec![bpf, kill_bpf]`; seccompiler 0.4.0's `apply_filter` is `prctl(PR_SET_NO_NEW_PRIVS)` + `seccomp(SET_MODE_FILTER)` (`lib.rs:347,363-364`), neither on the allow-list, so the second install gets EPERM — measured locally (one filter installs; allow→kill EPERM; kill→allow ok) and on CI (run #9 `runner-as-is-verbatim install=errno=1`, both images). P20 corrected: this host nests seccomp; the refusal is at install, before execve. (b) The candidate: kill-first order + Landlock Execute|ReadFile on program+loader+libs + six additions `poll, rt_sigaction, clone3, memfd_create, sendto, recvfrom` (leave-one-out: dropping `poll` or `rt_sigaction` → SIGSEGV 11; `clone3` → rc 101; `memfd_create` → rc 3; `sendto`/`recvfrom` → recall denied; `ppoll` proven unnecessary locally and on CI run #10) — the runner, hello-dynamic and hello-static all exit 0 under it on both CI images (run #9, steps 7–17 green). A forbidden syscall dies SIGSYS 31 locally (`q4b-forbidden-syscall-selected-candidate-local.log`) — 17-6 AC1's proven red exists. (c) Transport: granting `socket`+`connect` for a socket path lets the sandbox reach unrelated AF_UNIX and loopback AF_INET listeners outside its Landlock grants (measured, `q4b-socket-escape-surfaces-local.log`), and the CI candidate denies socket-path recall while five inherited-fd relay variants complete (`t6-ci-run9-t2-annotations.log`). (d) Kernel price: `linux.rs` +16/−1, `runtime.rs` +6, `worker_spawn.rs` +1, `j1.rs` +1, `cli_wrapper_bridge_8_12.rs` +1; the prototype applied and built `--locked` rc 0 (`q4b-kernel-price-prototype.log`). | OLD (17-6 AC2–AC3): "only the measured seccomp additions (P19: poll/ppoll/rt_sigaction)". NEW: a 1-line order swap (kill filter first), the **6** additions above (`ppoll` NOT needed), Landlock program/loader/libs rights, and the recall fd inherited per spawn (a connected socketpair end — the ADR-060 §D-C transport amendment proposed for ratification, RT-4), with a CLOEXEC clear before exec [INFERENCE: counted inside the `runtime.rs` +6, not separately measured]. | **`17-6`** |
| **R17-45** | **Q6 — only M3 reaches the proxy without reaching the internet (measured in CI on both runner images).** `egress/egress-probe.sh` ran on ubuntu-24.04 (podman 4.9.3, netavark) and ubuntu-26.04 (podman 5.7.0, netavark), throwaway run `36207588609`; run `36206431636` agrees. The two images gave identical annotations. M1 `slirp4netns:allow_host_loopback=true` → host **yes**, internet **yes**. M2 pasta → host no, internet **yes**. M3 `--network=none` + a `--volume`-mounted Unix socket + an in-container TCP→unix forwarder on 127.0.0.1 → host yes, internet **no**, `lo` up. The socket at 0600 is **refused** under the rootless user-namespace remap; at 0666 it connects. Rootless podman on the dev host cannot clone, so CI is the measurement. | OLD (17-1 AC1–AC4; ADR-061 `:59-61`, *a daemon-side proxy on host loopback*; R17-32): no mechanism chosen. NEW: **M3**. The gated literal `"--network=none".to_string()` (`t3/argv.rs:72`) stays. One `--volume=<daemon-owned socket>:/proxy/egress.sock` argv entry joins `:78`'s existing `--volume`. The forwarder ships in the signed T3 image, so `t3-image.lock` is re-signed. The socket is 0666 inside a daemon-owned 0700 directory [INFERENCE: the directory is the host-side custody boundary]. The escape corpus is unchanged. 17-1 AC4's proof asserts **internet unreachable** as well as proxy reachable. M1 and M2 are rejected. The ADR-061 wording *on host loopback* becomes a **proposed amendment** for operator ratification (RT-4): a `--network=none` container cannot reach host loopback by construction. | **`17-1`** |

## §R6 — the 17-6 preflight round-table (2026-09-26, HEAD `c0a397ea`)

Filed under rule 9 by the 17-6 preflight round-table (operator directive: *resolve the issues raised in the story creation for the long-term correctness*). Source: `implementation-artifacts/17-6-t2-sandbox-repair-and-proven-red.md` §Preflight round-table (RT-1…RT-10) and §Premise corrections P19–P22; evidence `implementation-artifacts/17-6-evidence/lean-*`. The 17-6 section's own OLD→NEW edits are applied by its dev pass in the landing commit; these rows re-shape **other** stories and are filed now.

| # | Finding | Lands on |
|---|---|---|
| **R17-46** | **17-3b is NOT kernel-Δ 0.** Under T2 the recall transport is an inherited socketpair fd (R17-44); its FD_CLOEXEC must be cleared in the child, which only `pre_exec` can do — `unsafe`, so only the sandbox zone may hold it (`lifecycle/cli_wrapper/runtime.rs` is `#![forbid(unsafe_code)]`). The runner's own allow-list entries — `memfd_create` (wasmtime; drop → rc 3), `sendto`, `recvfrom` (relay; drop → recall denied), all leave-one-out measured in 17-3a CI runs #9/#10 — also land with the story that proves them (17-6 RT-4: an entry lands with the story that proves it necessary). OLD: *kernel-Δ 0 (side-channel design, D-C)*. NEW: **◆ FLAG-Winston**, kernel figure measured by a prototype at 17-3b creation (E16-A7): the three syscalls + the inherited-fd hand-off on `SandboxSpec` (a field the bridge already carries after 17-6 RT-2) + its `pre_exec` clear. The ADR-060 §D-C transport amendment (inherited fd) was RATIFIED 2026-09-26 and is written into `docs/adr/ADR-060-spirit-forms-by-trust-tier.md` §Amendment. | `17-3b` |
| **R17-47** | **The `maos run` SIGSYS proof moves from 17-6 AC5 to 17-3b AC1.** ADR-060 clause 4 (ACCEPTED, `:76-77`) governs `[cli_wrapper]` by T3 admission, and ADR-031's T2 boundary belongs to the WASM runner, so the first production T2 child is 17-3b's runner. 17-6 lands and proves the T2 route at the primitive (`spawn_and_bridge`, T0–T4 table). NEW 17-3b AC1 clause: *a WASM component that calls a KillProcess-listed syscall, launched by `maos run`, dies of `SIGSYS` through `spawn_and_bridge` under the admitted T2 `SandboxSpec` — run id recorded.* | `17-3b` AC1 |
| **R17-48** | **A production T2 kill must be attested, at the primitive.** Today a `SIGSYS` death through the bridge becomes a crash (`ExitCause::Signaled { signal: 31 }` → `SignaledByKernel`) and **no** kind-8 `SandboxBlock` row, while `maos-bin/src/escape_detector_consumer.rs` waits for exactly that row. NEW 17-3b AC1 clause: *the bridge's exit classification emits `CapAuditEvent::SandboxBlock` for a T2 child killed by `SIGSYS` (`classify_exit` → `emit_sandbox_block`, `security/mod.rs:504`); `maos audit query --spirit <runner>` shows the row; the escape-detector consumer counts it.* | `17-3b` AC1 |
| **R17-49** | **The cookbook teaches third parties the first-party form.** `docs-site/docs/cookbook/hello-world-spirit.md` (block `:24-48`) walks an outside author through a `forms = ["rust-inproc"]` Spirit, which ADR-060 clause 1 reserves for code compiled into the daemon; `manifest-fields.md`'s reference block (`:17-155`) documents further fields the parser refuses [INFERENCE for all but `[resources]`, which 17-6 fixes]. 17-6 fixes only `[sandbox]` + `[resources]` (its AC6 lines). NEW: 17-3b, which ships the third-party form, rewrites the authoring cookbook around `wasm-component` and makes the reference block parse (en + ko). | `17-3b` (`deferred-work.md` row) |
| **R17-50** | **The T2 CI matrix is four images, including arm.** `aarch64-unknown-linux-gnu` is a shipped release target (`release.yml:46-48`) and `libc::SYS_poll` does not exist there; `ubuntu-24.04-arm` and `ubuntu-26.04-arm` are GitHub-hosted labels (`actions/runner-images` README, 2026-09-26). OLD (D6): *both `ubuntu-24.04` and `ubuntu-26.04`*. NEW: every job that proves **T2** (17-6's suite, 17-2's cgroup legs that spawn a T2 child, 17-3b's runner under T2) also runs on both arm labels, or T2 is refused typed on aarch64 until it does. T3/egress jobs (17-1) keep the two x64 images; whether T3 needs an arm leg is 17-1's own call at its creation. | `17-6`, `17-2`, `17-3b` |

## §R7 — 17-3b reshaped against the roadmap (2026-09-26, the 17-6 preflight round-table, operator directive: *reshape 17-3b, after contemplation, in alignment with and fulfilment of the roadmap*)

Filed under rule 9. Measured at HEAD `c0a397ea` by two scouts (roadmap; WASM code surface) and read against `prd/functional-requirements.md` (FR5 `:28`, FR29 `:75`, FR33 `:83`), `prd/project-scoping-phased-development.md:16-18` (Fork C: *FR5 holds by form; FR33 is served by WASM toolchains*), `RELEASE-HOLDS.md:28,32-38` and epics 18–21. The sections above are the result; these rows are the reasons, and the routing of every earlier row that landed on the old key.

| # | Finding | Lands on |
|---|---|---|
| **R17-51** | **The old `17-3b-wasm-third-party-form-with-log-recall` was three stories with different consumers, kernels and proofs; it is split and the old key retired.** Consumers: 20-1 AC4 and epic-20 exit lines 7–9 need only the form token, form-by-tier registry admission, the componentize-js artifact and a typed engine-off refusal (`epic-20…:20-22`, `:80`); **no story in Epics 18–21 needs execution or recall** (epic-21 §Dependencies dropped the `17-3b → 21-2` edge as vestigial). Kernels: the contract/admission half spawns nothing (Δ 0); execution needs `memfd_create`, a frame path through the bridge and a kind-8 emission; recall needs `sendto`/`recvfrom` and the inherited-fd hand-off. NEW order: **17-3b** (after 17-3a only — parallel with 17-6) → **17-3c** (after 17-6 and 17-3b) → **17-3d** (after 17-3c); **17-3b → 20-1**. Schema v5 is bumped by whichever of 17-1 / 17-3b lands first. Routing of the rows filed against the old key: R17-18, R17-28, R17-29, R17-30, R17-31, R17-40, R17-41, R17-49 → **17-3b**; R17-38 → **17-3b** (toolchain) and **17-3c** (release runner in exit line 2); R17-39 (a)(b)(d) → **17-3c**, (c) → **17-3b**; R17-43 → **17-3b** (the `@2.0.0` break) and **17-3d** (the open label); R17-24, R17-34, R17-47, R17-48 → **17-3c**; R17-23, R17-42 → **17-3d**; R17-46 → **17-3c** (`memfd_create`) and **17-3d** (`sendto`, `recvfrom`, the descriptor hand-off); R17-50 → **17-3c** and **17-3d**. | `17-3b` / `17-3c` / `17-3d` |
| **R17-52** | **The typed engine-off refusal has no code and no owner.** Epic-20 exit line 9 and 20-1 AC1(d) expect *"the typed engine-off refusal (WASM engine off in published binaries until Hold 2, 15-5 AC3)"* (`epic-20…:22`, `:77`, provenance `:34`), but 15-5 AC3 is a `STABILITY.md` statement only, and `grep -iE 'engine.?off' crates/` is empty; a default build simply sets `spirit_host = None` (`crates/maos-bin/src/main.rs:2359-2360`). NEW: **17-3b AC1/AC3** owns it at maos-bin admission (`WasmEngineOff`); epic-20's three cites move from 15-5 AC3 to 17-3b. | `17-3b`; epic-20 cites (applied) |
| **R17-53** | **FR33's scaffold is the template, not the example.** `templates/spirit-ts/manifest.toml:5,7,30` (schema 2, `ts-inproc`, `T0`) is the FR33 vehicle, and `examples/example-spirit-ts` is regenerated from it under a CI drift check (`xtask/src/templates_regen.rs:67-83`, `discipline.yml:1077-1078`) — rewriting only the example reds `templates-regen --check`. 20-1 AC4's `trust_tier = "public_untrusted"` edit to the example (`epic-20…:80`) hits the same drift: it goes through the template or the example stops being regenerated. | `17-3b` AC5; `20-1` AC4 (at its creation) |
| **R17-54** | **No production path spawns a class Spirit, and the bridge is not a frame bridge.** Every `LoadedSpiritKind` arm is in-process (`main.rs:444-468`, `:4472-4545`, `:4926-5477`); `spawn_and_bridge`'s only production caller is the Worker path (`worker_spawn.rs:803`); it journals stdout as lossy text (`runtime.rs:571-633`), never decodes or routes an ADR-032 frame, and closes stdin under `Signals` (`:487-493`). The old Design-in-force claim — *"the runner's stdio is the ADR-032 bridge … stays byte-identical"* — was false. | `17-3c` AC2 |
| **R17-55** | **The daemon compiles and instantiates untrusted components unsandboxed.** `WasmHostAdapter::resolve_launch` runs `conformance::probe_component` in-process (`crates/maos-wasm-host/src/adapter.rs:80-89` → `conformance.rs:45-51`: `Component::new` + `Spirit::instantiate`, which can run guest start code; the timed-out thread keeps running). ADR-031 keeps WASM execution out of process; a third-party form whose admission check runs the guest inside the daemon is not isolated by construction. | `17-3c` AC3 |
| **R17-56** | **R17-47's proof shape is not expressible.** A WASI guest has no raw syscall; the HEAD world imports nothing (`wit/spirit.wit:219-230`) and the WASI context is minimal (`host_state.rs:20-25`). The SIGSYS proof belongs to the runner process: a debug-only fault-inject feature (the house rule, `not(debug_assertions)` + `compile_error!`) driven with a Rust fixture that compiles under the watchdog in debug. | `17-3c` AC4 |
| **R17-57** | **The PRD still says WASM Spirits are T4.** NFR-Sec-1 *"v2.0 (T4 WASM)"* (`prd/non-functional-requirements.md:34`) and the v1.0 line *"Sandbox tier T4 WASM (tools, not Spirits yet)"* (`project-scoping-phased-development.md:188`) contradict ADR-031/ADR-060 (`:89`, *"WASM-component runner, T2 process boundary"*) and 17-6 RT-1 (T4 refused, typed). A third-party author following the PRD writes a manifest admission refuses. | ✅ **RESOLVED 2026-09-27 by the operator** (Lunarpulse: *do as the team suggested, for long-term correctness and alignment with the roadmap*): the PRD is amended at four sites under `[DELTA-2026-09-27 … R17-57]` — NFR-Sec-1 (`non-functional-requirements.md:34`), the sandbox-tier ladder (`domain-specific-requirements.md:42`), the capability table (`user-journeys.md:329`) and the v1.0 line (`project-scoping-phased-development.md:188`): **WASM-component Spirits run at T2 (ADR-031's process boundary) + WIT; T4 is reserved for the WASM *tool* sandbox** (`product-scope.md:50`), refused at admission until a story builds it. The code docs follow by rule 10(b): `crates/maos-domain/src/invariants/i9.rs:85` → **`17-6`** AC5 (it already rewrites `:79`); the ABI twin `crates/maos-spirit-abi/src/compliance.rs:228` (*"WASM-component sandbox (speculative-vNext)"*) → **`17-3b`** AC3. |
| **R17-58** | **"Admits `wasm-component` at ≥ `TrustTier::PublicUntrusted`" is ill-posed**: the enum orders `Local < OrgInternal < PublicVetted < PublicUntrusted` (`crates/maos-spirit-abi/src/compliance.rs:194-203`), so "≥" means one tier. NEW: every tier; the refusal is by form (`rust-inproc`), not by tier. | `17-3b` AC4 |
| **R17-59** | **FR62(b) was mapped to the old key and claimed by no AC** (`requirements-inventory.md:568`: *"the WIT extension is the first emitted event"*). The WIT break is now 17-3b's, so the claim is too. | `17-3b` AC2 |

Unit tests added under `src/` count; new tests go to `tests/`. | 17-1 |
| **R17-71** | **OLD (R17-5):** `spawn_and_bridge` already pipes; `spawn_t3` is unpiped; the return type is `SandboxedContainerChild`, not `Child`. **NEW (re-derived @`0d73cac3`):** the seam is `spawn_child` (`runtime.rs:513-532`, 17-6's) and `SpawnedBridge.child` is `Option<BridgeChild>` (`Plain`/`Sandboxed`); T3 needs a third variant `Container(SandboxedContainerChild)` (its `pub child: Option<Child>` exposes the piped stdio); `BridgeSpawnSpec` carries neither the `VerifiedImageAttestation` nor a `T3SpawnContext`, so it gains `t3: Option<T3Launch>` (surface pin `kernel-api-classes.toml:622-628` re-hashes; a new owned collection on a pub kernel struct trips `check-service-boundary`'s P3 — measured on the prototype: `T3SpawnContext.env`). `spawn_and_bridge` has a **second** production caller (`spirit_session.rs:263`, the 17-3c T2 runner). | 17-1 AC4 |
| **R17-72** | **OLD (R17-4):** the observer's pid is unsatisfiable through podman (another pid namespace; needs `--cgroups`/`--pidfile`). **NEW (ruled):** `child_pid` = the `podman run` **client** (`runtime.rs:559`) — attached it exits when the container's main process exits, and it is the daemon's child so `waitid(EXITED|NOWAIT)` works; the host pid from `inspect` is in the host namespace (the OLD wording was wrong) but is not a child (`ECHILD`) — informational only. Kill = `<runtime> kill <name>` + the guard's `stop`/`rm`. `spawn_t3` leaks the container when `inspect_container_host_pid` fails (the guard is built after it, `:120-126`) and a fast-exiting container can vanish before the ≈2.25 s retry window ends: guard first; the probe fixture holds ≥ 3 s. | 17-1 AC4(b) |

Under T3 the `Command` is the **podman client**; the container's environment comes only from `--env` flags (`t3/argv.rs:66-86` has none, nor `-i`). `env_clear()` on the client strips `PATH`/`HOME`/`XDG_RUNTIME_DIR` that rootless podman needs [INFERENCE until the `/proc` test runs]. NEW: `--env=NAME` (never `NAME=VALUE`: argv is world-readable to the operator uid), values on the client's environment, `--http-proxy=false`, client allowlist; `env_clear()` at the one `Command` build for the other tiers (also the T2 WASM runner; it reads no env). | 17-1 AC3 |

## §R11 — the 17-2 preflight round-table (2026-10-11, HEAD `b19556ab`)

Filed under rule 9 by the 17-2 preflight round-table. The operator's directive was *resolve the issues raised in the story creation for the long-term correctness*. The operator ruled FW-01 (including `journal/mod.rs`), FW-02/03 (*no raise; exact at landing*) and FW-07 (new story 17-7) on 2026-10-11 (`implementation-artifacts/17-2-flag-winston-decision.md`). | # | Finding (OLD → NEW) | Lands on |
|---|---|---|
| **R17-109** | **NEW (RT-4):** `JournalAdapter::append_transition` releases the writer mutex (`journal/mod.rs:188`) before updating the `most_recent` index (`:189-195`), so concurrent same-Spirit appends can leave the live index out of file order. 17-2 adds the first such pair: the session thread appends the downgrade while the pidfd observer appends `Crash`. **Ruled (FW-01):** fixed at origin in 17-2 by deleting `drop(file);`. Proof is a barrier-synchronized stress test; the pre-fix red is probabilistic. | 17-2 T4 |
| **R17-110** | **OLD (story):** *terminal SCB state stays terminal* across reopen. **NEW (RT-5):** no boot-time SCB rehydration exists. The daemon builds an empty SCB map (`scheduler_loop.rs:132-167`). `recover_in_flight` returns every identity, crashed ones included (`journal/mod.rs:206-212`), and the only production recovery call counts task rows (`main.rs:6678-6702`). `ProviderSwitched = 18` is already an informational row. A future replay consumer must reduce state-changing events, never treat the raw last event as liveness. | 17-2 AC2.6, tests |
| **R17-111** | **OLD (§17-2 *CI decision*, AC1 recipe):** root-level `sudo mkdir … echo $$`. **NEW (RT-3):** the hosted precedent is a **system-manager** `sudo systemd-run --unit … --uid --gid -p Delegate=yes` (`discipline.yml:1878-1882,3328-3334`), not `--user`. The 17-3c helper enables only `+cpu +memory` (`run_17_3c_delegated.sh:21-26`). 17-2's helper must assert that `pids` is available and enable `+pids`. | 17-2 AC1, T5 |
| **R17-112** | **NEW (RT-6):** 17-2 was marked `ready-for-dev` with no FLAG-Winston record, but the 17-1 FW-05 precedent keeps a story in `backlog` until its charter is ruled. **Ruled 2026-10-11:** `17-2-flag-winston-decision.md` covers FW-01…FW-08, and the status is retained. ## §R12 — 17-7 creation (2026-10-13, HEAD `37c34be4`)

Filed under rule 9 at 17-7's creation. Five read-only scouts (kernel T3, maos-bin supervision, tests/CI/docs, upstream podman/conmon/crun, governance) re-read every §17-7 cite; one agent built the E16-A7 prototype in an isolated copy; three non-author validators checked the story. The operator ruled `17-7-flag-winston-decision.md` on 2026-10-13 (variant A; FW-02…FW-09 as proposed); the rows below record each ruling.

| # | Finding (OLD → NEW) | Lands on |
|---|---|---|
| **R17-117** | **OLD (AC1):** *an intentional `exit(137)`, an external SIGKILL and a `memory.max` OOM stay distinguishable.* **NEW:** not from Podman. conmon writes `128+WTERMSIG` (`ctr_exit.c` v2.1.10/v2.1.13 `:100-107`), `InspectContainerState` has no signal field (podman v4.9.3 `:208-232`, v5.7.0 `:298-322`), the `died` event carries only the integer, and `StoppedByUser` is set by any podman kill. The payload's own wait status is readable for a non-child via a pidfd and `PIDFD_GET_INFO`/`PIDFD_INFO_EXIT` (Linux ≥ 6.15; runners 6.17/7.0, R17-104); the prototype read `Exited(137)` vs `Signaled(9)` on kernel 7.2.9. **Ruled 2026-10-13 (D-17-7-FW-01): variant A** (pidfd, 4 `unsafe` sites in the existing `sandbox/linux.rs` zone); runtime-only evidence is the per-spawn fallback. AC1 stands as written. | 17-7 AC1, FW-01 |
| **R17-118** | **OLD (AC2):** *an owned `--pidfile` replaces the `State.Pid` polling.* **NEW (confirmed, made concrete):** an exited container reports `State.Pid = 0`; `--pidfile` is a `create` option written by the OCI runtime during `podman init`, before the workload exec gate opens, and not removed at exit; `podman create` prints the CID before start. Lifecycle: `create` → `init` → read pidfile → `start --attach <cid>` (the supervised client). No polling remains. | 17-7 AC2 |
| **R17-119** | **NEW:** the runtimes do not protect a stale identity file — crun truncates (`container.c` 1.14.1 `:1939-1946`), runc renames over, podman's `CreateIDFile` truncates (`utils.go` v4.9.3 `:1104-1114`). Freshness is MAOS's job: a private `0700` per-spawn directory; the CID comes from `create` stdout, not `--cidfile`. | 17-7 AC2 |
| **R17-120** | **OLD cites → NEW @`37c34be4`:** guard drop `runtime.rs:503-510` → `:948` (the old range is the T2 cgroup accessor); `FaultCause` beside `maos-domain/src/supervision.rs:63-85` → enum `:92-114`, `OomKilled` `:109` (`:62-85` are accessors); `--rm` `t3/argv.rs:77-106` → `:74`; named kills `runtime.rs:481-496` (`.status()` `:485`), `supervision.rs:914-919` → `:906-941` (`.status()` `:923`); `on_unload` `:1979-1987` → `:1985-1993`; classifiers `:731-745` → `:734-747`, `:1813-1838` → `:1820-1845`; client-argv helper `worker_supervision_16_3.rs:1819-1853` → `:1819-1854`; root helpers `root_shutdown_unload_16_3.rs:285-324` → `:222-468`; fixture hold `worker-cli-fixture.rs:41-45` → `:44` (calls `:92,121`); probe hold `:80-95` → `:81-88`. | 17-7 story |
| **R17-121** | **NEW:** `OOMKilled` is conmon's marker on `memory.events` `oom` **or** `oom_kill` — hierarchical, victim-less; shipped 2.1.10/2.1.13 use one shared counter with an early return (`cgroup.c` `:268-314`/`:269-315`), unlike current main. No production path constructs `FaultCause::OomKilled` today; T2's `oom_kill` count writes only a `SandboxBlock` row (`runtime.rs:965-971`). The story fixes an authority contract: `fault.oom_killed` only when the marker coincides with SIGKILL (variant A) or runtime code 137 with unknown payload status; a marker with a clean exit changes nothing. | 17-7 AC3 |
| **R17-122** | **NEW:** the two "classifiers" are two feeds into one executor (`supervision.rs:1820-1845`); the observer can write `task.orphaned` before the pump reaches EOF, so a finalization-only inspect is too late. One snapshot, computed once by whichever feed asks first, with its own ≤ 2 s deadline (the 10 s `runtime_call_bounded` default would endanger FR12's ≤ 5 s orphan bound). | 17-7 AC3 |
| **R17-123** | **OLD (`deferred-work.md:1126`):** *a hung runtime client can exceed the 30 s hook budget.* **NEW:** the budget times out the await on `spawn_blocking` (`hook_dispatch.rs:572-589,665-687`) and never cancels the blocked command; mechanism (x) already releases SCB/receipt/capabilities before the hook (`scheduler_loop.rs:527-583`); the comment at `supervision.rs:816-821` claiming otherwise is stale. The door unload (`operator_door.rs:524`) reaches the container **only** through the hook, so a hook that panics before its stop leaves the Worker running until root stop: 17-7 adds a post-unload registry stop on every Worker unload path. The existing unload-panic test is an in-process `HookSpirit` (`loaded_spirit_exit_16_6.rs:30-45,255-307`), not a container. | 17-7 AC4 |
| **R17-124** | **OLD (AC4):** *the lease ends at confirmed payload termination (`worker_spawn.rs:770-798`).* **NEW:** that range is TTL/credential setup; acquisition is `:803-812`. Release is `WorkerBinding::finish` (`supervision.rs:1113-1120`) or `WorkerEgress::drop` (`egress.rs:181-187`) — on an abandon path the binding drops **before** the bridge (`LiveWorker`, `:1159-1162`), so today the lease ends before the container is killed. `WorkerEgress::finish` (`egress.rs:160-178`) has no *termination unconfirmed* state, so 17-7 adds one (revoke now, retain and report). | 17-7 AC4 |
| **R17-125** | **NEW:** a retained container stores its bearer in `Config.Env` (`--env=NAME` resolves from the `create` client, `specgen.go` v4.9.3 `:442-478`); `podman-restart.service` restarts only `always`. **Ruled 2026-10-13 (D-17-7-FW-07):** explicit `--restart=no`, no MAOS start of a CID it did not create, report-only residue via `maos purge`; **no** boot-time destructive sweep — roots may share one data home (`egress.rs:189-191`), and ADR-067 keeps purge report-only (`:87-90`). | 17-7 AC4 |
| **R17-126** | **OLD (AC5):** *two helpers, two fixture holds, cookbook EN `:71` / KO `:72`.* **NEW:** five T3 inspection holds (add `worker_completion_2a.rs:837`, `sandbox_t3_spawn_linux.rs:47`, `t3-network-escape-probe/src/main.rs:27-28,43`); six simulated-runtime tests pin `kill <name>` (`worker_supervision_16_3.rs:815-855,889-907,912-1152`); the report constructor and wire assertion (`maos-control/tests/post_surface_16_1.rs:38-57,930-934`); five T3 launch constructors, including the direct smoke `main.rs:7084-7108` and `t3_network_escape_executor.rs:93-98`; cookbook EN `:77` / KO `:78`, `4-kernel-design.md:337`, `5-spirit-abi.md:130-131`. The resource probe's 5 s + 2 s retention (`worker-cli-fixture.rs:260-276`) is 17-2's live-cgroup evidence, **not** a hold — keep it. | 17-7 AC5 |
| **R17-127** | **NEW:** the exit block had no 17-7 command, and `scripts/epic_17_exit_line1.py` verifies only lines 1/1b. Exit line 4 is added (above). The OOM scene needs the delegated rootless envelope where 17-2 proved `memory.max` (`cgroup-ceiling-delegated`, `discipline.yml:3246-3253`); `aggregate` has no explicit success predicate for `epic-17-exit` (the cgroup-job pattern is `:5176-5181,5342-5346`). | 17-7 AC1, T6 |
| **R17-128** | **NEW:** the FR4 classifier matches the `cli.subprocess.exit` writer shape exactly (`maos-audit/src/fr4_classifier.rs:396-409`, matcher `:589-604`) and a drift fails closed to `Call`. 17-7 keeps the native four-key shape byte-identical and adds a second exact five-key T3 shape with a typed `container` object — never `container:null` on native rows. `maos-audit` is an exact zero-headroom pin (7341, `kloc.toml:404`). | 17-7 T2 |
| **R17-129** | **NEW:** runner runtime versions are neither pinned nor logged (`provision-t3-test-substrate/action.yml:17-22` installs podman if absent). Package baselines: noble podman 4.9.3, conmon 2.1.10, crun 1.14.1; resolute podman 5.7.0, conmon 2.1.13, crun 1.21; the active OCI runtime is unknown. 17-7 logs the actual versions (evidence only). Separately, upstream advisories CVE-2026-57231 (image `Env` imports client env; fixed 5.8.4/6.0.0) and CVE-2026-55686 (WORKDIR traversal; fixed 5.7.1) fall in both baselines and are **not** fixed by 17-7: **ruled 2026-10-13 (D-17-7-FW-09)** — bounded by signed-image-only execution; the production host's runtime version belongs to `ops-live-worker-under-17-1-profile` (deferred-work row). | 17-7 T6, FW-09 |
| **R17-130** | **OLD (§17-7 *Closes · Δ*):** *scout estimate +120…+360 physical lines [INFERENCE].* **NEW (measured, `17-7-evidence/measurements.json`):** variant B +291 `src_lines` / +326 tokei; variant A +429 / +454; `maos-domain` +47 tokei; files 95 → 95. Includes 79 lines of probe scaffolding and omits a create→start CID guard, typed residue and docs. Out of kernel every affected row is an exact zero-headroom pin (domain 9349, bin 25396, audit 7341, egress 1769, aggregate 173500 vs strict 173501) → measured requests before landing. **Ruled 2026-10-13 (FW-02/FW-03):** kernel ≤ 25574 physical / ≤ 19946 tokei (variant A), exact lower figure at landing; out-of-kernel exact at landing. | 17-7 delivery gate, FW-02/03 |
| **R17-131** | **NEW:** `SandboxInspectReport.pid` is captured once at startup and never refreshed (`t3/child.rs:50-73`; SCB `control_block.rs:366-375`); nothing uses it as a kill target today (kills use the client pidfd and the name). The report becomes `container_id` + a historical `payload_pid`. `record_sandbox_application` promises the exact report in the lifecycle journal (`scheduler_loop.rs:232-253`), but maos-bin passes the scheduler adapter, whose projection drops the payload (`supervision.rs:1681-1687`; `scheduler_loop.rs:735-754`): **fixed at origin by 17-7 AC2.4** (rule 10(a); maos-bin threads the shared `JournalAdapter`, no kernel change). | 17-7 AC2 |
| **R17-132** | **NEW:** 17-2's CI-execution route (D-17-2-FW-08) authorizes `ci/17-2-*` branches only. **Ruled 2026-10-13 (D-17-7-FW-08):** `ci/17-7-<purpose>` under the same conditions. | 17-7 T6, FW-08 |
| **R17-133** | **OLD (R17-119 / story AC4.1):** CID recovery depends only on successful create stdout. **NEW:** Podman commits `ContainerCreate` before writing `--cidfile` and stdout (`create.go` v4.9.3 `:181-195`). A fresh private, bounded regular-file CID receipt is the sole cleanup authority and preserves it when the client fails after that write; normal success requires receipt/stdout agreement. Missing/invalid receipt means creation-ownership-unknown residue even with a valid-looking stdout CID; never remove by name or stdout alone. Conflicting stdout never authorizes deleting a second container. | 17-7 AC2/AC4, T1/T4, PF-01 |
| **R17-134** | **OLD (story AC4.3):** every stop, including cleanup of unexpected failure, latches PlannedStop. **NEW:** that suppresses the sole ECHILD finalization fault (`supervision.rs:1176-1179,357-366`). Only intentional stops latch; mechanical fault cleanup preserves the original classification. Both observer-first and ECHILD/client-first cases must prove exactly one Truncated disposition and no completion after cleanup succeeds. | 17-7 AC3/AC4, T3/T4, PF-02 |
| **R17-135** | **OLD (§Classification):** unknown payload with any runtime C becomes NonZeroExit{C}. **NEW:** only ambiguous valid `129…=192` codes use that fallback (137+OOM remains the prior special case). Missing, malformed, negative or >255 evidence without valid pidfd status is Truncated; Podman `-1` is a missing-exit-file sentinel (`container_internal.go` v4.9.3 `:150-173`), not a payload exit. Raw runtime values remain separate. | 17-7 AC1/AC3, T2/T4, PF-03 |
| **R17-136** | **OLD (T2):** “exact” nested container schema is unnamed for failures. **NEW:** story §Exit-row schema declares six required keys, nullability, allowed payload/source pairs and removed/residue invariants; legitimate success/fallback/unavailable/residue rows must pass both FR4 renderers (`fr4_classifier.rs:589-604`; audit `lib.rs:642-650,887-903`). Native/historical four-key rows stay unchanged. | 17-7 AC3, T2/T4, PF-04 |
| **R17-137** | **OLD:** one immutable unavailable snapshot and later stop confirmation had no explicit handoff to the lease. **NEW:** classification facts stay immutable; cleanup confirmation advances separately and governs lease finalization. Successful cleanup never upgrades completion; unconfirmed death keeps revoked, reported lease residue through Drop/root cancellation (`egress.rs:35-53,160-187`). | 17-7 AC4.5, T3/T4, PF-05 |
| **R17-138** | **OLD (AC4.4):** “kill all before confirming any” alone establishes the 17 s N-worker bound. **NEW:** current `stop_workers` executes serially (`supervision.rs:1537-1543`). All teardown stages must remain in flight across CIDs under one root absolute deadline; the five-second idle grace / forced-exit path (`:2006,2315-2347`) cannot bypass cleanup/reporting. N≥3 delayed/hung stubs and concurrent finalizer/Drop prove the bound and one verdict per CID. | 17-7 AC4.4, T3/T4, PF-06 |
| **R17-139** | **OLD:** pin ENTRYPOINT while retaining the old argv tail; numeric PID and listed State fields lack explicit bounds/types. **NEW:** `/maos/spirit` occurs once (`argv.rs:100-103` tail becomes `IMAGE -- <command>` with pinned entrypoint); PID fits positive pid_t; locked libc ABI and returned EXIT bit govern pidfd decoding. Each State field's type is required; empty Error/zero-time strings remain legal. Preserve stop-before-attachment semantics (`supervision.rs:666-685`) with a behavioral race oracle. | 17-7 AC2/AC3/AC4, T1/T3/T4, PF-07 |
| **R17-140** | **OLD:** completion uses “authoritative classification” without specifying voluntary exit 0; helper deadline covers synchronous reaping/drains. **NEW:** Voluntary `CrashCause::exit_code()` is None (`maos-domain/src/supervision.rs:70-74`), so completion must use the verified payload status directly. Helper return is deadline-bounded even when pipes/reaping remain unresolved, which is typed runtime-client residue, not claimed success. Real immediate-exit completion and descendant-held-pipe controls remain mandatory. | 17-7 AC3.6/AC4.4, T3/T4, PF-08 |

## §R13 — 17-3d creation (2026-10-17, HEAD `39cd7966`)

Filed under rule 9 at 17-3d's creation. Four read-only scouts (recall stack and audit, WASM host and T2 spawn path, planning/governance, previous stories) re-read every §17-3d cite; one agent built the E16-A7 prototype in an isolated copy (variant A transport, variant B + the grant literal). Rows marked *pending* were **ruled 2026-10-18** as proposed (`17-3d-flag-winston-decision.md`: charter ratified; FW-07 A, FW-08 scaffold, FW-09 A — R17-156).

| # | Finding (OLD → NEW) | Lands on |
|---|---|---|
| **R17-141** | **OLD (AC1):** *fetching another Spirit's frame id returns `recall-error::scope-violation`; exact rows fixed at creation.* **NEW:** `maos run … --once` feeds one inbound frame, the operator seed, delivered at pid 0 (`spirit_session.rs:102-128`, `:356-361`); no second Spirit exists. The vector is `fetch(seed.frame-id)` → `ScopeViolation` (`log_recall.rs:481-487`). The recall-operation rows at the TS pid are exactly, as an ordered subsequence: R1 `capability.invocation`/`log.recall`, R2 `capability.invocation`/`log.fetch` (both token-bearing), R3 `governance.event`/`log.fetch.refused:scope-violation` — the governed issuer's `identity.asserted` rows may interleave under SSO (`worker_spawn.rs:374-398`); line 2b exits 0. Own-frame recall *content* is proven by the multi-turn suite, not the `--once` line. *Pending D-17-3d-FW-06.* | 17-3d AC1, FW-06 |
| **R17-142** | **NEW:** exit line 2b had no `- Line 2b` provenance bullet, so `check-exit-commands` could not attribute an owed token to 17-3d (`check_exit_commands.rs:777-837`). Added above, naming 17-3d AC1. | exit block (applied), 17-3d T9 |
| **R17-143** | **OLD (R17-23):** *the channel binds the caller pid per spawn.* **NEW:** not sufficient across runs: the TL is persistent (`transparency_log.rs:511-535`), the pid allocator restarts at 1 per process (`scheduler_loop.rs:29-33`), `FrameFilter` has no boot field (`transparency_log.rs:213-236`) and `fetch` compares only the pid — a Spirit at pid N reads another Spirit's rows from an earlier run at pid N today, native Researcher included. Local `recall`/`fetch` bind `(boot nonce, pid)` at origin (rule 10(a)); cross-boot recall by a verified stable identity is out of scope. *Pending D-17-3d-FW-04.* | 17-3d AC3.4 |
| **R17-144** | **NEW:** `query_page` applies `LIMIT` before dropping kind-7 rows and intent mismatches (`log_recall.rs:320-344`), and `apply_cursor` then ends the walk (`:295-301`); every recall/fetch writes a kind-7 row at the caller's pid, so frequent recallers lose their frames. The predicates move into SQL before `LIMIT`. | 17-3d AC3.4 |
| **R17-145** | **OLD (AC3):** *checks `Scope::LogRecall` against the grant (`cap_policy/mod.rs:185-188`).* **NEW:** `:185-188` is the approval arm; the gate is `:135-172` via `issue_with_mediation`; no manifest syntax grants `LogRecall`/`LogFetch` (`manifest.rs:676-688`, `:796-838`); `LogFetch` is distinct (`i1.rs:82-85`). New `[capabilities.required.log]` `recall`/`fetch` on schema 5 (17-3c precedent, no bump), ratified `17-3d-log-capability`; each call minted through `issue_enterprise_governed_capability`. Kernel: one `CapabilitiesRequired` literal (`scheduler_loop.rs:330`). *Pending D-17-3d-FW-05.* | 17-3d AC3.3, AC3.5 |
| **R17-146** | **NEW:** the adapter's call rows are tokenless (`log_recall.rs:370-377`, `:453-460`) and the FR4 `--spirit` view rejects them (`maos-audit/src/lib.rs:605-610`; `fr4_classifier_16_2.rs:100-111`); a failed fetch writes no outcome row; the plain view prints intent, not payload. Attributed adapter entry points carry the minted token id; every operation returning `Err(recall-error)` writes one kind-28 `log.<op>.refused:<code>` (an empty page is a success and writes none). The native path's tokenless rows are a latent FR4 violation: **ruled 2026-10-18 (D-17-3d-FW-09 A)** — one mediation path for both forms, through the Researcher's injected port, Researcher manifest to schema 5. | 17-3d AC1, AC3.3, T6 |
| **R17-147** | **OLD (AC3):** *`sendto`/`recvfrom` with leave-one-out proofs* (read as SIGSYS). **NEW:** the T2 default action is EPERM (`linux.rs:378-387`); SIGSYS is the hostile kill list only. Prototype: omit `sendto` → EPERM/exit 72, `recvfrom` → EPERM/exit 71, CLOEXEC clear → EBADF/exit 9. Proofs assert the guest-visible `unavailable` with the real recall relay on the four images; an echo-only runner is not a red (`q4b-seccomp-leave-one-out-local.log:90-108`). | 17-3d AC3.6 |
| **R17-148** | **OLD (AC4, R8):** *lossless frames let recall honor consent.* **NEW:** the TL never persists envelopes (`adapter.rs:496-499`, `:584-595`; `transparency_log.rs:194-209`), the adapter's check is a comment (`log_recall.rs:489-497`), and guest-emitted frames are restamped with no envelope (`spirit_session.rs:948-960`): under emitter scope no WASM-recallable frame carries one, so AC4 has no positive vector. **Ruled 2026-10-18 (D-17-3d-FW-07 A):** safety vector + reserved `recall-error::consent-withheld` in 17-3d; `17-8-participant-recall-and-consent-envelopes` owns participant recall + persisted consent for both forms (R17-156). | 17-3d AC4, 17-8 |
| **R17-149** | **OLD (AC2, R17-43):** *`@2.0` guests keep running on `@2.1` hosts (the Q5 matrix).* **NEW:** Q5 measured `@1.0/1.1/1.2` on Wasmtime 46.0.3; HEAD locks 48.0.6 (`Cargo.lock:6115-6118`) and the runner checks only the `frames` major (`runner.rs:325-338`). Fresh proof: a `@2.0.0` guest built at test time from a frozen copy of today's WIT runs on the `@2.1` runner, with and without `--recall-fd`. | 17-3d AC2.4 |
| **R17-150** | **OLD (R8):** *`wit_corpus.rs` (xtask) version binding; re-issue with the required `[toolchain]` table.* **NEW:** the test is `crates/maos-wasm-host/tests/wit_corpus.rs:221-261` (20 records today); the `[toolchain]` table exists (`equiv-fixtures.provenance.toml:14-18`) and is updated by the re-issue; the 4 pinned components stay 4 (the compat guest is built at test time, no fifth blob). | 17-3d AC2.3, AC2.5 |
| **R17-151** | **OLD (§Closes · Δ):** *kernel figure measured by a prototype at creation.* **NEW (measured, `17-3d-evidence/measurements.json`):** variant A (transport) +17 `src_lines` / +12 tokei; variant B (A + `scheduler_loop.rs` literal) **+18 / +13** (26311 → 26329, 20545 → 20558); files 94 → 94; two `fcntl` operations inside the existing `pre_exec` `unsafe` block, zero new blocks. `maos-manifest` 4466 → 4493 against pin 4474 (+19 over). Out-of-kernel rows exact at landing. *Pending D-17-3d-FW-01…03.* | 17-3d §Delivery gate |
| **R17-152** | **OLD (AC3):** *the Yama `ptrace_scope ≥ 1` condition is stated where deployment is documented.* **NEW:** the page is `docs-site/docs/deploy/topology.md` (+ KO twin; `gate-ko-coverage` checks only that the twin exists). ADR-060 states theft at `ptrace_scope=0` as measured, but the committed vector records only `1` (`q4-fd-vectors.log:5-9`): document it as a deployment condition, never as an executed scope-0 control. | 17-3d AC3.8 |
| **R17-153** | **NEW:** exit lines 2/2b run `examples/example-spirit-ts`, which `templates-regen --check` keeps identical to `templates/spirit-ts`, so the recall demonstration and `[capabilities.required.log]` land in the scaffold (*pending D-17-3d-FW-08*). The `@2.1.0` label is `FrameKindLabel`'s serde name (one spelling, unknown → `invalid-request`); the 17-3a WIT/host are reused for shape only (closed 30-case enum, unknown→`TelemetryEvent` fallback, OS-pid identity and u32+JSON framing are not ported). | 17-3d AC2.1, T5, T7, FW-08 |
| **R17-154** | **OLD (AC5, R17-42):** *`20-7` measures recall against its NFR-Perf-5 fixture.* **NEW:** 20-7 AC4 names only a 30-day audit-query fixture (`epic-20-ship-it-w5.md:189`); nothing obliges it to benchmark the WASM recall route. Filed on 20-7 AC4: the production route (host import → inherited descriptor → mediation → adapter) is measured on that fixture and reported separately from CLI audit-query latency; that leg needs 17-3d `done` and blocks neither Tier C nor the other benches. | `20-7` AC4 (applied), `dependency-dag.md:217` (applied) |
| **R17-155** | **NEW:** the scheduler's recall seam is concrete (`Arc<LogRecallAdapter>`: `scheduler_loop.rs:110,127-128` → `hook_dispatch.rs:118,167` → `kernel_ctx.rs:27,85-86`) and **dormant** — nothing reads `KernelCtx.log_recall`. FW-09 A therefore mediates the Researcher's injected `Arc<dyn LogRecallPort>` (`main.rs:5349-5352`) and leaves the kernel seam untouched; a future reader takes the mediated port through a charter request. Researcher declares schema 4 (`spirits/researcher/manifest.toml:31`), so its log grants need schema 5. | 17-3d T6, FW-09 |
